Round 5, 2026-11-18: a fresh session given the fifth draft at `ea38897`, the program plan at its matching commit, the private port catalog and this repository. It built nothing. It checked each round-4 action against the body and the program plan, re-checked eleven earlier actions the fifth draft touched (all intact), probed the parser and git's push routing in throwaway repositories, and recomputed the budgets. In a scratch clone, `selftest`, `check` with the private deny list, `check --phase ci` and `grade.py --recorded` passed, and 214 unit tests passed with 2 skipped. Marks: **V** means a file, a command or a computation was checked; **I** means inferred. The verdicts are decided. The sixth draft applied each action to the body, as noted in each row.

Sixteen of the 20 round-4 rows are fully applied. R4-5, R4-7, R4-8 and R4-10 are only partly applied; R5-15, R5-14 and R5-8 finish them. There is no blocker. Four findings are new defects (R5-3, R5-5, R5-6 and R5-12), so this round does more than finish earlier work, and a sixth draft and a round-6 check come before approval. The loop is converging: new defects fell from about nine in rounds 3 and 4 to four here. The three guard findings share one cause, guards that list command forms instead of resolving the real target, and R5-6's action replaces the lists with a resolution rule so the next round has no new form to find. No row needs the maintainer's yes.

| # | Finding | Verdict | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| R5-1 | The retry path's range, "about 920 to 1,010", passes the 1,000 stop, and it undercounts probes: that path has nine recording sessions, not seven, so it is about 925 to 1,030 (V, computed from the plan's figures). The stop could fire in the middle of R2 or the routed arm. The base path with all three re-records fits, at about 874 to 971 (V). | Accepted | Keep 1,000. On the retry path, before R2 starts, the session projects the remaining runs; if the projection passes 1,000, the plan stops for the maintainer's decision and starts no recording it cannot finish. Restate the retry range as about 925 to 1,030. Applied: key points, Stop rule. |
| R5-2 | Dispatched sessions count toward the 1,000 but not clearly toward the 150-run session cap. B7's adversarial-review recording is 132 runs plus up to 132 dispatched sessions in one session (V, Limits, B7, stop rule). | Accepted | Limits counts dispatched sessions toward the 150. B7 records adversarial review one case per session, about 140 runs each with dispatches and probes. Applied: B7, Limits. |
| R5-3 | `building:` names one milestone, but the program runs M5 alongside M4 and M7 while M6 waits (V, program headings). One milestone overwrites the other, and between milestones the field is empty. Program step 1 says the first slice sets it; the plan says A3, which comes after A0 (V). | Accepted | `building:` is a list. Each milestone adds itself when its first slice merges and removes itself at sign-off; every milestone, instance milestones included, edits this repository's manifest. The check refuses a `prove-by` newly set to any listed milestone. Program step 1 changes to match. Applied: A3, criterion 14; the program's step 1. |
| R5-4 | One failed attempt licenses every later move: nothing ties the record to the milestone skipped, so debugging could move from M4 to M11 before M4 starts (V, key points, A3, criterion 14, program M4 slice 8). | Accepted | A move later needs a failed-attempt record naming the page and the milestone it moves away from, made while that milestone was in `building:`. Criterion 14 gets a case: an old failed attempt does not license a second move. The program's M4 wording changes to match. Applied: key points, A3, criterion 14; the program's M4. |
| R5-5 | `gh api` writes can reach unlisted repositories. `GH_REPO` only fills `{owner}/{repo}` placeholders, so `GH_REPO=<this repo> gh api repos/<upstream>/… -f …` resolves as listed (V, `gh api --help`). A path with no owner/repo falls through to this repository (I). GraphQL node ids are global and mutations are refused only on unlisted repositories (I). R4-20's approval rule misses GraphQL reviews and `--input` or `@file` events (V, plan text). | Accepted | A `gh api` target is the path's literal owner/repo; placeholders resolve through `GH_REPO`, then set-default, then the remote. A write whose path names no repository counts as unlisted. GraphQL mutations are refused on every repository, this one included. On a reviews path, `--input` and `@file` fields are refused, and `event` must be a literal other than APPROVE. Criterion 39 gets a case for each. Applied: C3, C5, criterion 39. |
| R5-6 | Git's config can rewrite where a push goes. In throwaway repositories, `-c url.<B>.pushInsteadOf=<A>`, `git config remote.origin.pushurl <B>` and `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT/KEY/VALUE` each sent a push of `origin` elsewhere (V, probe). `url.*.insteadOf`, `remote.*.url`, `remote.pushDefault`, `branch.*.pushRemote`, `include.path`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `git remote add` and `set-url` are not named (V, grep). No part guards edits under `.git/` (V, grep of `parts/`), so `.git/config` can be written directly (I). | Accepted | Replace the form lists with a resolution rule. At hook time the force-push and outward-write guards work out the push's real destination URL and refspecs as git would, from the resolved folder's config plus the command's `-c` and `GIT_CONFIG_*` values. They refuse `-c`, `git config` and environment writes to `remote.*`, `url.*`, `branch.*.pushRemote`, `remote.pushDefault` and `include.*`; refuse `git remote add` and `set-url` to an unlisted URL; and count `gh repo set-default` or `git remote` earlier in the same command line as unresolved. The edit guard refuses agent edits under `.git/`. A local-path remote follows git's rules: `host:path` is ssh. Criteria 38, 39 and 55 get a case each. Applied: key points, A0, C3, C5, criteria 38, 39 and 55. |
| R5-7 | The force-push guard misses `--all` or `--branches` with `--force`, `--delete` or `-d main`, `--prune` with a glob refspec, glob refspecs, and refspecs supplied through `xargs` (V, grep and parser probe). Main's branch protection already refuses force pushes and deletions (V), so the guard is defence in depth, which matters most for instances. | Accepted | C5 and criterion 38 cover these forms through R5-6's resolution rule. Arguments that `xargs` or `find -exec {}` supply count as unresolved for the guard that decides on the command. Applied: A0, C5, criterion 38. |

## Approval

Approved by: the maintainer, 2026-11-18, on the eighth draft. The maintainer's words are paraphrased in this plan, never quoted.

## Changes after approval

### 2026-11-18

Approved by: the maintainer, 2026-11-18

A demotion needs an approved change too. A side review during slice A0 found that, under A3 as written, one edit from `proof: proven` to `proof: unproven` takes a page out of the eval gate, and no check refuses it. Criterion 14 gains: a proven page or section marked unproven again, or stripped of its mark, fails unless an approved entry here carries a line `Demote: <page>`, the same form as the anchor rule. The maintainer agreed the same day. Decision record 0006 states the rule, and the `proof` check enforces it with a case.

### 2026-11-21

Approved by: the maintainer, 2026-11-21

Slice C3 is too large for one reviewable pull request. It holds seven separate pieces of machinery: the shell facts, the target resolver, the agent tools' settings and their check, the worktree settings copy, the discard guard, the secret scan with its CI job, and the commit-subject check, plus a message rule across every part. Slices C1, C2 and C7 each merged as one pull request of 616 to 818 added lines. Several of C3's pieces are each that size, so one pull request would run to about 3,000 lines (inferred, an estimate from the row's parts). Slice C3 stopped after its decision record 0004 amendment, which stays its opening step.

The change splits C3 into four slices, each its own branch and pull request at the driver tier. Nothing in the row or its criteria is dropped or weakened.

| Slice | Work, from C3's row | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| C3a, shell facts and the discard guard | The 0004 amendment. The runner adds `session`, `lease` and `calls` to a before-shell event, with each git call's working copy. The discard guard as a new part, and the 0005 amendment on worktree sessions. | Criterion 31; criterion 64's third sentence; criterion 58 except the target repository |
| C3b, the target resolver | Each gh call's and push's target, from every source the row names, with `pushurl`, `insteadOf`, `pushInsteadOf`, `push.default` and the upstream modelled. No guard reads it until C4 and C5. | Criterion 58's target repository, from each source |
| C3c, settings | Allow and deny lists for both agent tools, the check that fails on a missing deny entry, the `post-checkout` copy of the local settings file, and both `.gitignore` entries. | Criterion 29; criterion 64's first two sentences |
| C3d, the commit | The secret scan, at the commit and over the whole history in CI. The commit-subject check, reading the manifest's formats. Every finding names its file, the rule's page and a fix or re-run command. | Criteria 32, 33, 34 |

C3a goes first, since its amendment opens the slice. C3b, C3c and C3d each start from main once C3a merges, and may run in parallel. C4 needs C3a. C5 needs C3a and C3b.

Proposed revision of criterion 34 (template rule 7). `no_stage_all`'s refusal names no page, and `parts/no_stage_all.py` is a file the eval hashes. C3d's test exempts that one message by name. Slice Z adds the page to it and removes the exemption, and the edit joins the list queued for slice Z.

### 2026-11-21

Approved by: the maintainer, 2026-11-21

The private port catalog gained a part on what changed in the source between 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-20: fifteen items and eight lessons. The maintainer settled where the items go. This entry records that. No approved section is edited, and no criterion is dropped or weakened.

**B3, the bug ticket standard.** B3 builds it in two layers, from the catalog's new description, in place of the nine-heading shape the catalog first described. The top layer is plain language for a product manager, a tester or a triage meeting: a short summary, then four fixed headings. The lower layer sits in one collapsed section for developers. The catalog's changed rules for the title, impact, acceptance criteria and out-of-scope headings come with it. The worked example is an invented ticket on the order system, since this repository has no tracker.

**B3, a reader standard.** B3 gains one more page. Every page kind a person reads names its reader, opens with a top layer under a word budget, uses fixed headings in a fixed order, and gives one worked example. The bug ticket standard is its first instance. It binds pages written from B3 on; retrofitting pages already here is not part of this change.

**B3, budgets for plans.** A Key points block holds at most 200 words. A table cell holds at most 60 words. Slice detail that does not fit a cell goes under a heading per slice, below the table. A new check enforces both budgets, built in B3 at the driver tier, as `claim_marks` is. It reads only plans with `status: draft`. Approved, done and abandoned plans keep their sections frozen, as template rule 6 says, and the check skips them. A word is a run of characters between spaces. `docs/plans/decision-queue.md` is a draft, so it must meet the budgets: the pull request that adds the check trims it, because `check` must pass on that commit.

**B3, decisions made without asking.** The plan standard and the pull request standard each gain a section with that name. It lists each choice the author made on their own, so a reviewer can see and challenge it. The plan standard is `hand-off-plan.md`, which B3 already merges into; the eval does not hash it (verified: none of the three eval cases covers it), so B3 edits it directly and nothing is queued for slice Z.

**B2 gains two procedures, both unproven.**

| Page | `prove-by` | Why that milestone |
|---|---|---|
| Search every source before writing: the workspace first, then one read-only helper per outside source, then a merged report | M8 | Its outside sources need the providers M8 connects. |
| The big-picture pass of architecture review: restate the problem, test its framing, try removals, name at most one simpler alternative | M6 | A section of the architecture-invariant review page, with its own proof line, at that page's milestone. |

The search procedure gets a B6 wrapper with the four selection cases the catalog names: two that must fire and two that must stay quiet.

**B4 gains four product lane drafts.** Triage labels on a bug, the triage meeting run from those labels, the changed grooming of a bug, and the changed rules for pages written for a product reader. Each carries `lane: product` and, where it is a playbook or how-to, `proof: unproven` with `prove-by: M9`, as B4's row sets.

**M4's, not this plan's.** The review orchestrator's proof that the architecture pass ran, and the change to who may accept a declined review finding. Both change M4's review machinery. Nothing in M3 builds them.

**Machinery, checked before anything is built.** Four items: a merge script that works from a throwaway worktree; one script that states when a branch's CI counts as passed, with a slow third leg that does not hold a merge; a session-start notice when main's CI is red; and three small changes to eval reruns, suite selection and the link check. Before C4 starts, a driver-tier session compares each with what this repository already has, and records the result under "Results". Anything it would add needs its own dated entry here first. One conflict is already visible (inferred, from the text alone): the slow-leg rule lets a merge proceed before the third system passes, while `AGENTS.md` requires CI to pass on all three. Changing that would edit a file the eval hashes, so it would also be queued for slice Z.

New success criterion:

68. The plan budgets check fails a draft plan whose Key points block holds more than 200 words, and one with a table cell over 60 words. It passes a draft at exactly 200 and 60. It skips an approved, a done and an abandoned plan over both budgets. `docs/plans/decision-queue.md` passes it. Each has a case. (V1, V2)

This settles the merge-script item of the second change of 2026-11-21. No criterion is dropped or weakened.

### 2026-11-21

Approved by: the maintainer, 2026-11-21

**C4's merge helper merges on GitHub, not from a local worktree.** This amends the entry above that has the helper merge in a throwaway worktree. That design cannot merge a machinery branch: the pre-push check refuses the push of a local merge commit that brings machinery in, as the machinery comparison under "Results" shows. So `ws.py merge` checks that every check the branch rule requires passed on the pull request's exact head commit, then runs `gh pr merge --merge --match-head-commit` with that commit. The merge uses no working copy, so the lease and the calling working copy play no part in it. Afterwards the helper deletes the branch on GitHub and brings the main working copy up to date, as row C4 and criterion 35 still ask. That step follows M2's session-start fast-forward: it moves only a clean main, and it is skipped with a note while another live session holds the lease. No criterion is dropped or weakened.

Slice C5 is too large for one reviewable pull request, as C3 was. Its row holds at least eight separate pieces of machinery: the after-edit event with the chain reminder, the stop gate, the handoff loader, the guard on `.git` and git config writes, the force-push guard, the outward-write guard with its repository list, the secrets guard with `ws.py with-secrets`, and the two approved additions above. C3's four pull requests added 4,884 lines in all, the largest 2,899, and C4's added 2,183 (verified, `git diff --shortstat` of each merge). C5's row is longer than C3's, so one pull request would run to about 6,000 lines (inferred, an estimate from the row's parts). Slice C5 stopped after its recorded tool runs, which the row puts before building.

The change splits C5 into six slices, each its own branch and pull request at the driver tier. Nothing in the row or its criteria is dropped or weakened.

| Slice | Work, from C5's row | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| C5a, after-edit and stop | The 0004 amendment for both events. `after-edit` in both adapters, with the chain reminder. The `stop` event and the stop gate; the ledger records each entry's session; the 0005 amendment. | Criteria 37 and 41; criterion 57 for the stop gate |
| C5b, session-start notices | The CI notice. `ws.py handoff`, its marker and the loader, with the 0004 amendment for the loader. | Criteria 59 and 69 |
| C5c, the git folder and git config | Writes under a `.git` segment and to git config files refused, by edit tool and by a shell write the parser reports. Config writes to A0's keys, alias expansion, unresolved subcommands, and `xargs` or `find -exec` arguments. `no_admin_merge` adopts the rule. The README's limits. | Criterion 55, except the parts below |
| C5d, the force-push guard | The force-push guard, the branch-name push message, and the earlier-in-the-line forms. The push guard's worktree extension. | Criterion 38; criterion 65 for this guard; criterion 55's alias that force-pushes main; criterion 36 as amended below |
| C5e, the outward-write guard | `outward-repos.json` and its refusals, the gh read list, the `gh api` rules, and pushes and remotes to an unlisted repository. | Criterion 39; criterion 65 for this guard; criterion 55's list redirect, alias to an unlisted repository, and `git remote add` or `set-url` |
| C5f, secrets | Environment dumps and secrets-file reads refused, and `ws.py with-secrets`. | Criterion 40 |

C5a goes first, since its amendment opens the slice; it starts from branch `m3/c5`, which holds the recorded runs. C5c follows, since C5b, C5d and C5e reuse its rules on config writes and aliases. C5b, C5d and C5e each start from main once C5c merges, and may run in parallel. C5f starts from main once C5a merges.

The maintainer approved the split as written, with criterion 69 and the criterion 36 sentence, answering Q12 in the workspace queue. The maintainer set the build order: C5a, then C5c, then C5b, C5d and C5e, then C5f.

Two criterion changes go with the split, since the approved additions above have no criterion:

69. At session start, when the newest CI run on main failed, a notice gives the number of failed runs in a row, when the first of them ran, the failing steps and the link. **B3 opens with two changes, before any other page.** Both are guidance pages and get an instruction review each.

1. The instruction review gains a severity mark, on its page and in its record format. The reviewer marks each finding blocking or not; the builder cannot lower it. Pages written from B3 on link the page instead of restating it, and the review counts a restated rule as a finding. A batch's shared-interface sheet keeps only what is local to that batch. B2's pages switch to the link when a slice next edits them.

These come from B3's row, which already holds the authoring conventions and the pull request standard; they move to the front.

A fresh re-check reads every blocking fix and every page holding a changed shared rule, and rules on every blocking finding the builder declined. The rule fires only when a blocking finding survives the fix round, a decline of a blocking finding is rejected, or a fix creates a blocking finding. A non-blocking finding is fixed once in the round. What remains goes to a "Known defects" section of the page's review record, for the page's `prove-by` milestone or the docs audit. A missing `INDEX.md` row still does not count. The plan's other stop items, the limit of two fix rounds on the same failure among them, are unchanged. The routed arm in slice Z (criterion 54) still measures whether the unproven pages harm the proven one.

**B2's remainder moves to slices with a gate of their own.**

A new slice, KILLFIX, from main's red CI after B3's second resume: C6's kill test failed once on macOS with Python 3.9, and the maintainer had the failed job rerun. KILLFIX has no row in the slice tables; this entry is its scope. No criterion is dropped or weakened.

**Lane C runs KILLFIX after PSFIX, then C8,** at the driver tier.

**C6's kill test measures the check's run time instead of assuming it.** `test_a_killed_commit_check_leaves_nothing_behind` times one whole run of the commit check and places every kill point as a fraction of that one sample. When the sample runs slow, a later point falls after the check has ended, and the test fails though the check left nothing behind. A driver-tier session follows the fix-a-bug playbook, on its own branch and pull request, so that a kill point never falls after the check has ended. KILLFIX is done when the test holds under a slow sample that fails it today, and its report is under "Results".

## Results

### C1, launcher and baselines

Built on branch `m3/c1`, from main at `7ebfdcb`. It touches no file the eval hashes.

```
Fixed: hooks, git hooks and CI now start Python only through wslib/launch.sh, once per call, in UTF-8 mode; a check can tolerate a recorded list of findings and still fails on any new one.

verified  criterion 26: tests/test_launcher.py fails on any wiring entry, git hook or CI run step that starts python, python3 or py by name; it failed on all four git hooks and six CI steps before the change, and passes after
verified  criterion 27: a counting stand-in python3 records one start for a before-shell and a before-handoff call through the real wiring; the old launcher's probe made it fail first
verified  criterion 57: through every wiring entry of both agent tools, with no Python, a Python reporting 3.8, a crash (SIGSEGV) and a failed start, the three guard events exit 2 and both session events exit 0; pre-commit, commit-msg and pre-push refuse, post-commit passes
verified  criterion 30: tests/test_baselines.py, on the links check: two baselined findings pass; fixing one and adding another fails, naming the new one; the same at check --staged, which reads the staged baseline, and at check --phase ci --changed-from; a second copy of a baselined finding fails; an unreadable baseline tolerates nothing and is an error
verified  the old wiring form, launch.sh hook before-shell, still runs ws.py, so a running session is not locked out when its main copy fast-forwards
verified  a before-shell hook call through the launcher: median 41 ms against 50 ms with the old launcher, 15 calls each, on the maintainer's Mac
verified  python3 ws.py selftest, python3 ws.py check and python3 -m unittest discover -b tests (240 tests, 3 skipped) pass; evals/grade.py --recorded finds no difference
inferred  Windows: the stand-in interpreters are shell scripts, so the start-count and failure-mode tests skip there; the launcher itself runs every Windows CI step, and a test fails when CI's Python differs from the matrix row's
inferred  criterion 57's stop gate: none is wired yet. The test requires every wired event other than the three guard events to pass when the launcher fails, so the stop gate C5 wires is held to it without a new test

Not done: the stop gate itself (C5). Index rows for the launcher and baselines, queued below for slice Z.
```

Fix after merge: a side agent found that a pull request could add a finding and its own baseline entry, and CI would pass; only this repository's test that every baseline is empty stopped it here. An entry now tolerates a finding only when the base has it too. verified: tests/test_baselines.py fails before the fix on a finding and its entry added in one change (folder, commit and CI forms), on an entry recorded ahead of its finding, and on a missing base, and passes after; a first baseline for findings main already has still passes CI.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **No baseline files are shipped.** A missing file tolerates nothing, and a test holds that every baseline here tolerates nothing. One empty file per check would have tied this slice to sub-batch A, which adds checks and retires `freshness`. `python3 ws.py baseline` writes a file when a team needs one.
- **The baseline matches path and message, not the line**, so a finding that moves with an edit above it stays tolerated. Each entry tolerates one finding.
- **A too-old Python exits 97 from `wslib/start.py`**, and the launcher tries the next one. So a machine whose `python3` is old and whose `python` is current still works, at the cost of a second start on that machine only.

### C2, the case runner

Pull request #22, at the driver tier. Criteria 28 and 42 are met.

- **Verified:** `tests/hook_cases.py` runs 27 cases from `tests/hooks/` across all five events the engine knows, for Claude Code and Codex. Each case builds a scratch repository and runs the wiring command from `.claude/settings.json` or `.codex/hooks.json` through `wslib/launch.sh`. Seeded leases use a live process and a dead one. Every `session-start` case runs against a local bare remote. All 27 pass on all five CI jobs, before and after merging C1's launcher into the branch.
- **Verified:** CI prints every row's median on all three systems. In the run after C1's launcher merged, before-tool calls and the two rows take 65 to 100 milliseconds on Linux, 77 to 130 on macOS, and 158 to 239 on Windows. `session-start` takes 101, 173 to 213, and 354 to 457. On Windows with Python 3.9, four rows are over the 200-millisecond budget; Windows only reports, as criterion 28 allows.
- **Verified:** `tests/test_hook_cases.py` shows the budgets fail on Linux and macOS and only report on Windows. It also shows a case with a wrong exit code, lease holder or output text is reported as failing, and a missing event or timing row fails the run.
- **Verified:** `test_cases_bite` reads the 20 parts from the manifest. Every part's cases fail against both stubs. It passed on its first run, so no part's cases were missing teeth. A test of the test shows that a part whose cases only allow, or only find, is caught.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` pass locally, 254 tests after the merge.
- **Inferred:** the session-start fast-forward already fetches under a timeout (`FETCH_BUDGET`, 15 seconds, in `wslib/lease.py`), so this slice left it unchanged. Against a slow real remote, a session start can still take up to 15 seconds. The 2-second budget holds only against the local remote the cases use, as criterion 28 says.
- **Proposed revision (template rule 7):** `session-end` has no budget in criterion 28, so the runner prints its time and holds it to nothing. It measures 63 to 202 milliseconds. A later plan could give it the 2-second session-start budget.
- **Verified:** two fix rounds, each on a different failure. After C1 merged, CI starts the tests from Git bash, where `git` resolves under `mingw64\bin`, and the runner's Git bash lookup failed on Windows. It now walks up from `git` to the folder that holds `bin\bash.exe`.
- **Verified:** a second failure, on one Windows job: a lease held by a dead process was refused as live. **Inferred** cause: Windows reuses a process id quickly once no handle to the exited process is open, so the "dead" id belonged to a new process. The runner now keeps the exited process's handle open for the whole run. `LeaseTests` in `tests/test_core.py` picks its dead id the same way and may flake on Windows for the same reason.
- **Inferred:** the same lookup in `tests/test_core.py` now finds no shell on Windows CI, so its wiring tests skip there. Windows skips rose from 3 to 14 between the pre-merge and post-merge runs. The `shell` helper in `test_core.py` needs the same walk; that is outside C2.
- No file the eval hashes was edited, and `workspace.json` was not touched. No question had to be asked.

### C7, clones and checkouts

- **Verified, criterion 44:** `tests/test_pins.py` runs `ws.py pins` on a fresh clone of a fixture workspace. It checks out the reachable pin. It skips a pin whose remote is gone, with a note. It leaves a pin already checked out, on its own branch with uncommitted work, exactly as it was. Three remotes that hang are probed at once and skipped at a 2-second limit, in under 5.5 seconds in all. A folder with files but no checkout, and a pin with no URL, are left alone.
