- **Verified:** with `backticked` on for `AGENTS.md`, `INDEX.md` and `README.md`, two paths fail, and each is exempted with its reason: `.claude/worktrees/` in `AGENTS.md`, which git ignores, and the README's worked example `parts/no_empty_pages.cases.json`. On every page, the same reading finds 32, nearly all in plans and milestone records that name files still to come or since moved. So the setting stays on the three entry pages.
- **Verified:** a cold clone of this branch from a local clone ran the README's marked steps, `selftest`, `check` and the tests, all passing.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check`, `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (454 tests, 3 skipped) and `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` (27 of 27) pass locally.
- **Verified, criterion 66, the pull-request half:** `gh pr checks 26` shows `cold-clone` passing on Linux and macOS, beside the five `check` jobs. `tests/test_cold_clone.py` holds the job's paths to `SETUP_PAGES`. **Verified, the push half:** the merge's push to main ran `cold-clone` and it passed (`gh run list --workflow cold-clone --event push`, run 44911353929). **Pending:** a pull request that changes no setup page, and so does not run it.
- **Verified:** one fix round. Two `tests/test_pins.py` tests failed on both Windows jobs: they deleted a fixture remote, and Windows refuses to delete git's read-only object files. They now move it away instead.
- **Pending, criterion 43:** three consecutive scheduled nights, the last after slice Z merges. A scheduled run tests main only, so the nights start after this merges.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The job runs the machine's own `python3`, with no checkout action and no setup-python.** It tests what a newcomer has. The README's steps start `python3` by name, so this is the one place CI starts Python other than through the launcher; the job's own steps still use the launcher, and the launcher test still passes.
- **Linux and macOS only.** A Windows install of Python provides no `python3`, so the README's steps would fail there for a reason the README already implies. The README's limits say so.
- **Setup pages are `README.md` only for now**, listed once as `SETUP_PAGES`. C8's kit adds its own pages to that list.
- **An exemption that tolerates nothing fails.** The plan asked only that a bad exemption fail. Without this, a fixed link would leave its exemption behind, silently widening the check later.
- **`ws.py pins` exits 0 when it skips an unreachable remote**, and 1 only when a remote answered and the checkout then failed. A person without access to one repository can still set up the rest.

### C3, settings, guards and the commit

Built as four slices, as the change of 2026-11-21 split it, each at the driver tier, from main, touching no file the eval hashes. The slice first stopped after its 0004 amendment, as the stop rule for a slice too large to review asks.

**C3a, shell facts and the discard guard.** Pull request #27, on branch `m3/c3` from main at `598d756`.

- **Verified, criterion 31:** `parts/no_discard.cases.json`, 87 cases. While another live session holds the lease, each form C3 names is refused in the main working copy and passes in a linked worktree: hard reset, `reset --keep` and `--merge`, whole-tree checkout and restore, `checkout -f`, `switch -f`, `read-tree -u --reset`, a forced clean, a stash write, rebase, cherry-pick, a rebasing pull and autostash. `stash drop`, `pop`, `clear` and `worktree prune` are refused from the main copy and from a worktree. `worktree remove --force` of another worktree or the main copy is refused, and of the session's own passes. `cd <main>; git reset --hard`, `git -C <main>`, `GIT_DIR` and `--work-tree` naming the main copy are refused from a worktree. With the lease free, held by this session or held by a dead session, the forms pass.
- **Verified, criterion 64's third sentence:** `git pull --rebase --autostash` in the main copy is refused to a session that does not hold the lease, in a part case and in a real call (`tests/hooks/before-shell.json`).
- **Verified, criterion 58 except the target repository:** `tests/test_shellfacts.py`, on throwaway repositories with a linked worktree. The working copy's kind is right for the session folder, `-C`, `--git-dir`, `GIT_DIR`, `--work-tree`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, a leading `cd` and a leading `pushd`. A non-leading `cd`, a `cd` in a subshell, `core.worktree`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and a `cd` to a variable give no fact, and the guard refuses a call with no fact. The session and its own working copy are given; the lease reads `free`, `mine`, `other-live`, `other-dead` and `unknown`, and reading it never writes it.
- **Verified:** nine real calls through the wiring and the launcher, with seeded leases held by a live and a dead process. Five of them, the refusals, failed with the manifest from main and pass with the part. All 36 hook cases pass; before-shell's median is 64 ms locally against its 200 ms budget.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` pass locally, 467 tests, 3 skipped. `tests.test_cases_bite` passes: `no_discard`'s cases fail against both stubs.
- **Verified:** three fix rounds, each on a different failure. On Windows CI the new test wrote folders with backslashes into shell commands, which the parser reads as escapes; it now writes them with forward slashes, as an agent's shell command would, and `~` in a git folder is expanded. Two real-call cases passed a Windows folder unquoted, which no shell reads as that folder; they now quote it. On one macOS job, before-shell's median rose to 217 ms, over its 200 ms budget, because every shell command now started git to place its folders. The git facts are now worked out only when a hook reads them, so the guard with the lease free, held by this session or by a dead one starts no git process; `LazyFactsTests` holds it.
- **Inferred:** a command a guard cannot judge is refused only while another live session holds the lease. A person working alone, or the lease holder, never sees this guard.

Decisions made in C3a:

- **Facts are keyed by the parser's own folder text**, not handed over as the parser's output. Cases stay short and still test the guard with the parser, and a key with no fact refuses. Decision 0004 records it.
- **A lease that cannot be read counts as held by another live session**, as decision 0005's "a lease bug never lets an edit through" asks.
- **Plain `worktree remove`, with no `--force`, passes.** git refuses to remove a worktree with changes unless forced.
- **Parser gaps handled in the guard's own file:** a whole-tree checkout or restore, a stash write, rebase and cherry-pick. The parser does not list them among the forms that drop work. Queued for slice Z below.
- **The maintainer kept the keyed design** after a side review noted it reversed the first draft of the 0004 amendment, which had the runner hand over the parsed calls.

**C3b, the target resolver.** Pull request #28, on branch `m3/c3b`, from main at `8de0aaa`.

- **Verified, criterion 58's target repository:** `TargetTests` in `tests/test_shellfacts.py`, 14 tests on throwaway repositories, no network. For gh: `-R`, `--repo`, `GH_REPO` on the command and in the environment, the set-default value, the only remote, two remotes with no default (unresolved), the folder after a leading `cd`, a repository operand, `gh api`'s literal path winning over `GH_REPO`, its `{owner}/{repo}` placeholders filled from `GH_REPO` and from the remote, and a gist, `gh api user` and GraphQL counted as naming no repository. For a push: two `pushurl` values give both destinations; `-c ... pushInsteadOf`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT/KEY/VALUE`, `remote.pushDefault` and a `pushurl` in an `include.path` file are all applied, by asking git; a URL on the command line gets `insteadOf` and not `pushInsteadOf`; a local path and `file://` are local and `host:path` is ssh; `remote.<name>.mirror` and configured `remote.<name>.push` refspecs are reported; with no refspec, `push.default` simple, current and upstream give the branch and its destination, so `-c push.default=upstream push -f` from a branch tracking main resolves to main; matching, `HOME` set on the command, and a variable remote, refspec, config key or folder are unresolved.
- **Verified:** the facts are worked out only when a hook reads them, as C3a's are. No guard reads them yet, so no hook call starts a git process for them. Measured directly on this repository, a resolved `git push -u origin HEAD` takes about 35 ms and a bare `git push` about 70 ms; C5's guards will add that to the push rows.
- **Inferred:** gh writes its set-default value under the remote's `gh-resolved` key. The tests set that key themselves; `gh repo set-default` refused a probe repository it could not reach.
- **Decision:** a target is keyed by the call's folder, program and words, as `target_key` gives them, so a guard in C4 or C5 can look it up from the parser's own output.
- **Verified:** CI passed on all five check jobs on the merged head commit.

**C3c, settings.** Pull request #29, on branch `m3/c3c`, from main at `8de0aaa`.

- **Verified, criterion 29, Claude Code:** `.claude/settings.json` denies `Read` of `.env`, `.env.*`, `*.pem`, `*.key` and `secrets/` anywhere in the workspace, and of `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.git-credentials`, gh's sign-in file and the private deny list's folder. The new `agent_settings` check, 13 cases, fails a missing deny entry in either tool, and `python3 ws.py check` passes on the committed settings.
- **Verified, criterion 29, Codex CLI's syntax:** `codex sandbox -c ...` with the same profile, `extends = ":workspace"` and each pattern `"none"` under `":workspace_roots"`, refused reading `.env`, `.env.local`, `sub/.env`, `*.pem`, `*.key` and a `secrets/` folder, and read a plain file. **Inferred:** that Codex loads the profile from `.codex/config.toml` in a trusted project; a deliberately invalid value there raised no error from this worktree, so the project file was not shown being read.
- **Inferred, criterion 29, Codex CLI's effect:** the profile is defined but not the default, as the maintainer chose on 2026-11-21. Making it the default would replace a person's own sandbox setting for this project, and `:workspace` turns off network access and writes to the home folder. So a Codex session denies the secrets files only when started with `-c default_permissions="agent-ready"`. The README and `docs/agent-settings.md` say so. Codex's profile reaches every process, so it holds no home folder entries: denying `~/.ssh` would also stop `git push`.
- **Verified, criterion 64's first sentence:** Claude Code allows `WebFetch` for eight documentation domains, and `agent_settings` fails a missing one. Codex CLI lists them in `allowed_domains` under `[tools.web_search]`; Codex accepted that setting on the command line and refused a wrong type for it. **Inferred:** that its web search then keeps to them; no recorded Codex run shows it.
- **Verified, criterion 64's second sentence, this repository:** `tests/test_settings.py`, on throwaway repositories with the real `.githooks/post-checkout`. `git worktree add` copies `.claude/settings.local.json` into the new worktree; a later checkout and a branch switch copy nothing; a missing file leaves the worktree add succeeding. `git check-ignore` shows `.gitignore` names the file. With the hook removed, every test in the file errors. **Pending:** the kit's `.gitignore`, which slice C8 creates.
- **Verified:** `tests/test_launcher.py` required every git hook to start the launcher. The new hook is plain `sh` and starts no Python, so the test now lets a named shell-only hook skip the launcher, and still fails it if it starts Python or names `ws.py`.
- **Verified:** CI passed on all five check jobs on the merged head commit.

**C3d, the commit.** Pull request #30, on branch `m3/c3d`, from main at `8de0aaa`.

- **Verified, criterion 32:** `secret_scan`, 14 cases, reads the lines a commit adds. `tests/test_commit_checks.py`: a staged line with a GitHub token fails `check --staged` without printing the token, and `check --history` fails on a token one commit added and a later one deleted, in a throwaway repository. CI runs `check --history` on every push and pull request; it reads 1,437 file versions here and finds nothing. The commit check now reads the last commit's copy of every changed file in its one batch read, not only pages.
- **Verified, criterion 33:** `commit_subject`, 14 cases, and `CommitSubjectTests` through `check --message`, the commit-msg hook's command. A conventional subject passes and one with no type fails; git's merge, revert and autosquash subjects pass; a subject over 72 characters warns; a `formats` setting in the manifest replaces the default.
- **Verified, criterion 34:** every part sets `PAGE`. The runner prints each finding with its page, when the message does not name it, and the command that runs the check again; a hook's refusal or reminder names its page too. `FindingsNameTheirPageTests` runs every part's cases and fails a message that lacks the file, the page or a fix or command. It found 77 problems before the change: parts with no page, and messages without their page or a command. Two parts are exempt by name: `no_stage_all`, whose file the eval hashes, queued for slice Z as the change of 2026-11-21 says, and `rule_pages`, whose reminder is the rule pages themselves, not a finding.
- **Verified:** the discard guard's stash refusal now says to set work aside with a commit on the session's own branch, not a stash.
- **Verified:** one fix round, on two failures in one CI run. The batch read asked for a submodule pin's old copy, which a newer git answers as `submodule`, not as text; pins are now left out. The message test needed a git identity, which a CI machine has not set.
- **Verified:** CI passed on all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on the merged head commit.
- **Fix after merge:** a side review found that one token in a merged commit, real or a look-alike, would fail the history step on every later pull request, with no way to accept it: history is never rewritten here, and the step took no baseline. Now `check --history` reads the branch's history, not every branch, and tolerates the entries in `baselines/secret_scan.history.json`, written by `ws.py baseline --history`, only when the base branch's history already has the finding. verified: four tests in `SecretScanTests` failed before the change and pass after: a revoked token's old commit is tolerated by a baseline against a base that has it; a token and its entry brought in one change still fail; with no base, nothing is tolerated; a token on another branch does not fail this one.

**Criteria, all four slices.**

```
verified  29: Claude Code denies reading the secrets files; agent_settings fails a missing entry in either tool (13 cases)
inferred  29: Codex CLI denies them only in its opt-in profile, by the maintainer's choice; the profile's syntax is verified, its loading from the project file is not
verified  31: no_discard, 87 cases and 9 real calls (C3a)
verified  32: SecretScanTests, staged and history; the CI history step passes here
verified  33: commit_subject, 14 cases; CommitSubjectTests through check --message
verified  34: FindingsNameTheirPageTests over every part's cases; no_stage_all waits for slice Z, rule_pages is not a finding
verified  58: working copy, session and lease (C3a), target repository from each source (C3b), in tests/test_shellfacts.py
verified  64: the allow lists (Claude Code verified, Codex CLI accepted, its effect inferred); the worktree copy and this repository's .gitignore; the rebase family with autostash refused (C3a). Pending: the kit's .gitignore, in C8
```

No question had to be asked that the workspace should have answered: the split, the keyed design and the Codex default each turned on the maintainer's choice. No file the eval hashes was edited.

**Proposed revision (template rule 7).** Criterion 64 names the kit's `.gitignore`, which does not exist until slice C8. C8's criteria should hold it: the kit's `.gitignore` names `.claude/settings.local.json`, and the kit carries `.githooks/post-checkout`.

### The machinery comparison, 2026-11-21

Made at the driver tier, as the second change of 2026-11-21 asks before C4 starts. Each machinery item in the private catalog's part of 2026-11-21, compared with this repository.

| Item | Here today | Result |
|---|---|---|
| A merge script that works from a throwaway worktree | Nothing: no `ws.py merge`. Agents run `gh pr merge`, and `no_admin_merge` refuses its `--admin` flag (verified, `parts/no_admin_merge.py`). | Approved for C4 in the change above. It conflicts with the push check; see below. |
| A CI status rule with a slow third leg | Nothing. | Not adopted, by the third change of 2026-11-21. |
| A session-start notice when main's CI failed | Session-start reminders exist, `fast_forward` and `stale_pins`; none reads CI (verified, `parts/`). | A new reminder part in C5. The maintainer approved it for C5 on 2026-11-21; C5's builder records that entry before building. |
| Eval reruns on a flaky failure; suite selection by every page a wrapper names | Neither exists. `evals/run.py` records arms per case, with no base-and-head suites and no reruns (verified, read). Wrappers arrive in B6. | Nothing added in M3. The private ledger maps both to M4's eval system. |
| The link check sees locally what CI sees | No suffix matching: a backticked path is read from the top of the workspace (verified, `parts/links.py`). But a local `check` and the commit check list the files inside a checked-out pinned repository (verified, read in `wslib/snapshot.py`), and CI checks none out (verified, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). So a link into a pinned repository can pass locally and fail in CI (inferred: this repository pins none, so it was not run). | Nothing added without its own entry. Queued for the maintainer, with M5's first instance, which has pinned repositories, recommended. |

**The conflict, which stops C4 before it builds.** Verified, in a scratch clone with a local bare remote: a branch changing `wslib/` was merged with a merge commit in a throwaway worktree of `origin/main`, and `git push origin HEAD:main` was refused by `direct_push`. The pre-push check judges the commits a merge brings in, and those change machinery. Inferred, from GitHub's documented branch rule: GitHub also refuses that push for anyone the rule binds, since the new merge commit has no passing checks. The maintainer is not bound here, because the rule does not apply to administrators (verified, the branch protection settings). So the approved design cannot merge without a change to the push check or a skipped hook, and neither is approved. The choice is queued for the maintainer with three options. The recommendation is to merge on GitHub with `gh pr merge --merge --match-head-commit`, after the helper's own check of the head commit's CI: it keeps the approval's aim, since no working copy is used at all. C4 waits for the answer. Its push guard and its `no_admin_merge` changes do not depend on it.


### C4, push and merge

Pull request #34, on branch `m3/c4` from main at `de8b4b3`, rebased onto `e154dd0` and merged as `ba28d30` by `python3 ws.py merge 34` itself, at the driver tier. The maintainer's answer to the queued merge question is recorded under "Changes after approval" first. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 35, the merge helper:** `tests/test_merge.py`, 36 tests, on `gh` answers recorded from this repository in `tests/records/merge-gh.json`, and four made-up answers the record names with their reason. It refuses a required check that failed, is still running, passed only on another commit, never ran, or ran from another app; the newest run of a check decides; a rule that requires nothing, a draft, a closed or merged pull request, a fork's branch and more than 100 check runs are refused, and none of these reaches `gh pr merge`. Otherwise it runs exactly `gh pr merge NUMBER -R HOST/OWNER/REPO --merge --match-head-commit SHA`, never with a squash, rebase, admin or delete flag, checks that the merge commit has two parents, and deletes the branch through the API. A head that moved fails the merge and leaves the branch. Of seven mutations of the helper and the fast-forward, six failed at least one test. The seventh, removing the strip of `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`, changes nothing, since `git config --local` does not read those variables (verified, a probe).
- **Verified, criterion 35, the fast-forward:** `FastForwardTests`, on throwaway repositories with a bare remote. From a linked worktree, a clean main moves to `origin/main`. While another live session holds the lease, the merge goes ahead and the fast-forward is skipped with a note, and main stays where it was. The caller's own lease, a dead holder, uncommitted changes, another branch and unpushed commits each behave as the session-start fast-forward does, and that fast-forward keeps its behavior; both now share one function in `wslib/lease.py`.
- **Verified, criterion 35, for real:** `python3 ws.py merge 34` merged this slice. It found the five required checks passed on `fd7230d`, merged it as `ba28d30`, whose parents are `e154dd0` and `fd7230d`, deleted `m3/c4` on GitHub, and skipped the fast-forward with a note, because the B2 session held the main working copy.
- **Verified, criterion 35, the refusals:** `no_admin_merge`, 50 cases, 31 of them new. It refuses `--squash` and `--rebase` in long, short and grouped forms, and set to true; a `gh api` merge whose `merge_method` is not `merge`, is a variable, or comes from a file or `--input`; a GraphQL merge or auto-merge with another method, a variable method, or a query read from a file; and an API write whose path is a variable. A body that looks like a flag, `--squash=false`, an API merge naming `merge` or no method, and reads pass. A real call through the wiring refuses `gh pr merge 12 --squash`.
- **Verified, criterion 35, no redirect:** `RedirectTests`. With `GH_REPO`, `GH_HOST`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, `GIT_DIR` and helper-like variables set, the repository and every `gh` call are unchanged, and gh runs without `GH_REPO` and `GH_HOST`. `-R`, `--repo`, `--squash` and `--admin` after the number are refused by the command line, and `--root` naming another folder is refused.
- **Inferred, criterion 35, which `gh`:** the helper finds `gh` on `PATH`, which is an environment variable. An agent that can set `PATH` can run `gh` itself, so a fixed list of install folders would add breakage and no protection. The README states the limit, and the question is queued for the maintainer as Q10.
- **Verified, criterion 36:** `push_lease`, a new before-shell hook, 23 cases. While another live session holds the lease, or the lease cannot be read, a push from the main working copy is refused in every form tried, including `-C` and a leading `cd` from a worktree, and so is a push whose folder cannot be placed and a command it cannot judge. A dry run, a push from a linked worktree, a push in another repository, a free lease, the holder itself and a dead holder pass. Four real calls through the wiring and the launcher agree; the `git push` row's median is 75 ms against 200.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check`, `check --staged`, `check --phase ci --changed-from origin/main`, `check --commits origin/main..HEAD` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (562 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 41 of 41; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `fd7230d`, the first run.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** a fresh driver-tier helper reviewed the five guidance pages the slice changed and found 12 defects in 5 records: 5 contradictions, 4 enforcement gaps, 1 step, 1 trigger, 1 placement. Eleven were fixed, two of them in code: an API write whose path is a variable is now refused, and more than 100 check runs is now a refusal, not an error. One is queued for the maintainer.
- **Zero fix rounds.** One rebase: main gained B2's commits while CI waited, and the review index took both slices' rows.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The fast-forward after the merge stays.** The maintainer's answer moved the merge to GitHub and said no working copy takes part in it; row C4 and criterion 35 still ask for the fast-forward, so the entry recording the answer keeps it, under M2's conditions.
- **The required checks come from the base branch's rule,** classic protection and rulesets together, as red-team row 25 asked; a rule that requires none is refused rather than read as "nothing to wait for".
- **Only a check's newest run on the head commit counts, and only success passes.** GitHub also counts a skipped or neutral check; the helper does not, and the README says so.
- **Only the caller's Claude Code process is recognized as its own lease holder.** Under Codex any live holder holds the fast-forward back; decision 0005 says so.
- **The push guard judges pushes from the main working copy only,** as the row says. A worktree's push of the main copy's branch, or of every branch, can still publish the holder's commits. Decision 0005 and the README state the gap, and the question of extending the guard in C5 is queued for the maintainer as Q11.
- **The README's merge setting is a step of "Use it for your own team"**, not of "Try it", after the review found a fresh clone does not need it; it also tells the person to make main's rule require the CI jobs, which the helper needs.
- **The review was not run again after the fixes.** Each fix applies its finding's own remedy, with no new instruction beyond it.

Proposed revisions (template rule 7):

- Row C4 says the kit tells the person to allow merge commits only. The kit does not exist until slice C8. C8's criteria should hold it: the kit's setup carries the README's step 6 of "Use it for your own team".
- Criterion 35's last sentence should read "No environment variable that names a repository or host, and no argument but the number, changes which `gh` call it makes", unless the maintainer chooses fixed install folders for `gh`.

### C5, reminders and guards: stopped before building

Stopped after its recorded tool runs, on branch `m3/c5` from main at `e1d978a`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's two approvals for C5 are recorded under "Changes after approval" first. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, both tools offer both new events.** `tests/records/after-edit-and-stop.json`, on `m3/c5`, holds one recorded run each of Claude Code 2.1.330 and codex-cli 0.169.0, in a scratch clone of main. Each sent `PostToolUse` after its edit tool and showed the hook's additional context to the agent, which quoted it. Each sent `Stop`, took a block with a reason as a refusal, showed the reason, and sent `Stop` again with `stop_hook_active` true. So the row's inference about Codex holds, and criteria 37 and 41 need no revision.
- **Verified:** Codex's `PostToolUse` carries the whole patch under `tool_input.command`, as its before-edit call does, so the after-edit adapter can read paths the way the before-edit adapter does. Neither tool's `Stop` payload names the files the session edited, so the stop gate needs the ledger's session field, as the row says.
- **Inferred:** the slice is too large for one reviewable pull request; the estimate is about 6,000 lines. A six-way split is proposed under "Changes after approval", with criterion 69 for the CI notice and an amended criterion 36 for the push guard's extension, waiting for the maintainer's yes. It is queued as Q12 in the workspace queue.
- **Not done:** criteria 37 to 41, 55's guard refusals, 59, 65 and the two approved additions. The private ledger's C5 rows stay at slice C5, since nothing is built.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The recorded runs went first,** because the row makes them a condition for building and they decide whether the slice needs a revision for Codex. They cost one short run of each tool.
- **The 0004 amendment was not written here,** unlike C3's opening step. Since B1, a change to a decision record needs an instruction-review record, and C5a's pull request will review the amendment with the code it describes.
- **C5c comes before the guards that use it,** because the force-push and outward-write guards both need its rules on config writes and aliases. Building them first would put the same rules in two parts.

### C5a, after-edit and stop

Pull request #35, on branch `m3/c5a` from `m3/c5` at `0ac508d`, rebased onto main at `55c52a1` and merged as `03ebde1` by `python3 ws.py merge 35`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's approval of the six-way split (Q12) is recorded under "Changes after approval" first. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 37:** `tests/test_after_edit_and_stop.py` replays the recorded `PostToolUse` payloads of Claude Code and Codex CLI from `tests/records/after-edit-and-stop.json` through the real runner. With a chain that names `notes/`, each gets a `PostToolUse` reply whose `additionalContext` names the chain and its paths; with no chain naming the path, each gets nothing. `chain_reminder`, a new after-edit hook, has 13 cases: a file, a path under a chained folder, two chains at once, a linked worktree's own index, and the quiet cases. `tests/hooks/after-edit.json` calls both tools' wiring for real: 6 cases, median 55 ms against 200.
- **Verified, criterion 41:** the same test replays both tools' recorded `Stop` payloads in order. With the session's own entry in the ledger and a broken link, the first stop gets `"decision": "block"` with the check's failure, in under 20 seconds, and the second, `stop_hook_active` true, gets a `systemMessage` report and no block. A session with no entries of its own stops with nothing run: a marker the test run would write stays absent. Failing tests refuse too, and a passing check and tests stop quietly after running. The recorded runs show each tool taking exactly one block as a refusal, then sending one more stop and finishing. `stop_gate`, a new stop hook, has 11 cases; `tests/hooks/stop.json` calls both tools' wiring for real, 5 cases.
- **Verified, criterion 57 for the stop gate:** `tests/test_launcher.py` runs every wired event with no Python, an old one, a crash and a failed start; the new after-edit and stop lines end in `|| exit 0` and pass, the guards still refuse.
- **Verified, the ledger's session:** an entry an edit tool wrote is `{"path", "session"}`, an entry `ws.py ledger --add` wrote is the quoted path as before, and both are read. The same path from two sessions keeps an entry each, and a commit drops both. The gate judges each working copy that holds the session's entries, a linked worktree included.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check`, `check --phase ci --changed-from origin/main`, `check --commits origin/main..HEAD` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (586 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 52 of 52; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `e05d301`, the first run, on Linux, macOS and Windows.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the four guidance pages the slice changed and found 21 defects in 4 records: 15 contradictions, 3 triggers, 2 enforcement gaps, 1 step. Nineteen were fixed. Two index rows are queued for slice Z below, and one finding's remaining question is queued for the maintainer as Q15.
- **Inferred, the report's display:** both tools took the block as a refusal in the recorded run, but that run's hook replied nothing on the second stop, so no run shows either tool displaying the `systemMessage` report. The README and decision 0004 say so.
- **One fix round.** The ledger's new line form broke four tests that read the ledger file raw, and the wiring tests that iterated every adapter event; both now read through the ledger module and the tool events. One rebase: main gained B2's batch 3 while the branch was built, and the review index took both slices' rows.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The runner, not the part, refuses a stop only once in a row,** from the `stop_hook_active` flag both tools send. A broken or over-strict stop hook therefore cannot trap a session. On the stop after a refusal the gate runs again, so the report shows the failure as it stands.
- **A run that times out or cannot start refuses, like a failure,** and so does an edit ledger that cannot be read. Each refuses once only, so the cost is one turn.
- **The fast tests are `tests.test_cases_bite`, `tests.test_shell`, `tests.test_m3_a` and `tests.test_settings`,** named in the manifest: about 2.3 seconds here beside a 0.6-second check, inside the runs' 18 seconds. A test fails if they take over 10 seconds on Linux or macOS.
- **The gate runs in every working copy that holds the session's own entries,** not only the folder the session started in, since a session in the main copy may edit a worktree.
- **A session that committed every edit is not gated,** since a commit drops its paths from the ledger, as the row's "entries in the ledger" implies. The commit check ran at that commit; the fast tests did not. Decision 0005 and the README say so.
- **An entry with no known session keeps the old line form,** so a ledger an older engine reads still holds only lines it understands until an edit tool writes one with a session.
- **No new recorded tool run.** C5's recorded runs, made before building, are the runs criterion 41 names; the replay test drives the real gate with their payloads.
- **The chain-table parser moved into `wslib/markdown.py`,** so the `chains` check and the reminder read the table one way. Parts may already import that module.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C4.

Note for slices C8 and C9: the kit's wiring files carry the two new events, and C9's headless sessions in a scratch repository will meet the stop gate if they edit files while that repository's check fails.

### C5c, the git folder and git config

Pull request #36, on branch `m3/c5c` from main at `537f01d`, merged as `36c698f` by `python3 ws.py merge 36`, which skipped the fast-forward because B2's session held the main working copy, at the driver tier. No approval in the maintainer's list names C5c; the split's approval (Q12) was recorded by C5a. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 55, the git folder:** `git_folder`, a new before-edit hook, 26 cases, and `git_folder_shell`, a new before-shell hook, 68 cases. A write under any `.git` segment is refused by an edit tool and by every shell write the parser reports: a redirect into `.git/config`, `sed -i`, `cp`, `mv` of the folder or out of it, `ln`, a write inside a subshell, a substitution or `sh -c`, after a leading cd and after a cd the parser cannot follow. So are an `rm` of the lease file, a write to a linked worktree's `.git` file and to `src/*/.git/`, and a stale `index.lock`, whose refusal tells a person to remove it. Edits and shell writes to `.github/`, `.githooks/`, `.gitignore` and `.gitattributes` pass.
- **Verified, criterion 55, git config files:** a redirect or copy onto `~/.gitconfig`, the XDG file, the real file a symbolic link to it names, and a file an include names, inside or outside the repository, is refused. The runner's new `git_config` fact lists them, with the system file; `tests/test_git_folder.py` works it out on throwaway repositories, includes that do not exist yet among them.
- **Verified, criterion 55, config writes:** `git config` that sets, unsets, edits or removes a key A0 lists is refused in any scope by `git_folder_shell`, each key with a case, and `git config user.email` passes. On a form `no_discard` or `push_lease` decides on, `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, and an include by `-c` or `GIT_CONFIG_*`, are refused as config writes, from a linked worktree too.
- **Verified, criterion 55, aliases and unresolved calls:** the runner's new `aliases` fact reads each alias from config at hook time, only for a call whose subcommand is not git's own. A stored alias that resets hard is refused as a hard reset in the main copy, and one for `git status` passes; a stored alias that pushes a branch from a linked worktree passes; `git -c alias.push=status push` is judged as a push; a subcommand that is neither built in nor an alias is refused while the guard is active. Arguments `xargs` or `find -exec` supply make a call unresolved: `find -exec git reset --hard {}` and `find -exec git push` are refused, and so is a writer whose files they supply, and `find -delete`. `no_discard` has 18 new cases and `push_lease` 8. Four real calls read an alias from a config file through the wiring.
- **Verified, criterion 55, the idioms:** `cd "$D" && make` and the heredoc commit and pull-request idioms go ahead through every before-shell hook while another live session holds the lease, as real calls.
- **Verified, `no_admin_merge` adopts the rule:** 15 new cases. A program word that is a variable, text piped into a shell, `eval` of a variable, a gh subcommand that is a variable, arguments `xargs` or `find -exec` give `gh pr merge`, `gh api` or bare `gh`, and a `gh pr merge` argument that is a variable are refused; a merge body from a substitution and `xargs gh pr view` pass.
- **Verified:** `wslib/gitrules.py` holds the rules the guards share, for C5b, C5d and C5e to reuse, and decision 0004's table names each part that imports it. `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (604 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 70 of 70, before-shell's median 82 ms and before-edit's 75 against 200; `tests.test_cases_bite` passes; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `f71bea5`, on Linux, macOS and Windows.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the five guidance pages the slice changed and found 19 defects in 5 records: 9 contradictions, 6 enforcement gaps, 2 steps, 2 triggers. Eighteen were fixed, four of them in code: the system config file is listed, a config git cannot read is reported, a variable built from another is filled in, and the refusal of an unknown git subcommand says to run a `git-NAME` program by its own name. One waits for Q15: whether decision 0004's In short line may change. Two index rows are queued for slice Z below.
- **One fix round.** The first CI run failed on every system: the access test read decision 0004 before its rows were committed, and on Windows a test named an include by a path with no drive.
- **Inferred:** the criterion 55 parts that C5d and C5e hold are not built here: a stored alias that force-pushes main or pushes to an unlisted repository, a redirect onto the repository list, `git remote add` and `set-url` to an unlisted URL, and `git -c` and environment writes of a push's destination keys. `gitrules.config_write(call, every_guarded=True)` is the rule they call; no guard uses it yet.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A persistent `git config` write is refused by an always-on guard, a transient one by the guard it concerns.** `git config` rewrites a file every guard reads, for every later session, so `git_folder_shell` refuses it whoever holds the lease. A `-c`, `--config-env` or `GIT_CONFIG_*` value lasts one call, so only the guard that decides on that call judges it.
- **A `-c alias.*` value is expanded, not refused,** so `git -c alias.push=status push` is judged as a push, as criterion 55 asks.
- **A write whose target is a variable is judged by what the line shows,** not refused outright: the values the line set, filled in, the home folder, and a `.git` segment, a config file's name or `git rev-parse` in the text. Refusing every variable target would refuse ordinary temporary files in every session. The README states the limit.
- **A writer fed by `xargs` or `find -exec`, and `find -delete`, are refused always,** as the row says for the guard that decides on the command. The refusal says to name the files.
- **The guards do not refuse while the includes cannot be read,** since every write would then be refused; decision 0004 labels the exception and the README states it.
- **Aliases of two folders that differ make the call unresolved** rather than being judged twice, since that case is rare and fail-closed.
- **Parser gaps handled in `wslib/gitrules.py`:** `unlink`, `rmdir` and `shred` as writes, `find -delete`, and a relative write after a cd into a git folder that the parser cannot follow. Queued for slice Z below.
- **`core.hooksPath` and `core.fsmonitor` pass,** since A0's list does not name them, and the README says one command can turn off the hooks. Whether to add them is queued for the maintainer as Q16.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5a.

**Follow-up, 2026-11-22: Q15, decision 0004's In short line.** Docs only, committed straight to main from the main working copy, at the driver tier. The maintainer's answer to Q15 is recorded under "Changes after approval", dated 2026-11-22. No file the eval hashes was edited. It stopped under the stop rule after two fix rounds on the In short line; the line is committed, with two known overstatements queued as Q18.

- **Verified, the layout rule:** `docs/layout.md`, "The opening", says an amendment to a decision record may also edit its In short line, and does so when it changes what the line sums up. The same paragraph now says a frozen page's front matter is not frozen, and states rule 6 of the template whole.
- **Verified, decision 0004:** its In short line names the writes to a git folder and to git config files that the guards refuse, and a new dated Amended paragraph says so and labels a limit the review found. The question log has a row for Q15.
- **Verified, a guard gap the review found:** `git_folder_shell` does not read the `aliases` fact, so `git cf remote.origin.url URL` passes it when `cf = config` is stored. An agent can store that alias in a file of its own with `git config --file` and point git at it with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`; `no_discard` and `push_lease` refuse the call only while another live session holds the lease. Checked by calling each guard's `decide` on built events. Queued for the maintainer as Q17, with C5d, which edits the same guard for Q16, recommended.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers found 9 defects in 2 records: 7 contradictions, 1 step, 1 trigger. Eight were fixed in full or in part. One, a part's docstring, went to slice Z's queue below, with the rest of two others: an index row, and dating the in-place edits of decisions 0004 and 0005. One part was declined, with its reason. A fresh re-check found every layout fix held, and two new contradictions in 0004's new text, both fixed. A second re-check found both fixes held and two more overstatements in the In short line: the README does not list every git command that changes config unseen, and two writes the parser reports pass. Those are queued as Q18.
- **Two fix rounds,** both on decision 0004's In short line. The stop rule bars a third, so this follow-up stops there.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py check`, `python3 ws.py selftest` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` pass, and the port ledger check maps every item. No ledger row named slice C5c, so none moved.

### C5d, the force-push guard

Pull request #37, on branch `m3/c5d` from main at `f4599ba`, merged as `4613494` by `python3 ws.py merge 37`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's answers to Q16, Q17 and Q18 are recorded under "Changes after approval" first. Q18's docs went straight to main before building, as `f4599ba`. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, Q18 first:** decision 0004's In short line points to the shared rules for which writes are refused, and to the README for the known misses. The README's list of git commands that change config unseen gains `git push -u` and `git remote rename`, and the others the reviews found, and says it may not be complete. One review: 2 contradictions in 2 records, both fixed.
- **Verified, criterion 38:** `no_force_push`, a new before-shell hook, 91 cases. It refuses a force push or a deletion of the default branch, judged by what git would send: `--force`, `-f`, a `+` refspec, a `--force-with-lease` with no ref or naming it, `HEAD` while on it, `--all` and `--branches` with a force, `--mirror` written or configured, `--delete`, `:main`, `--prune` with a glob or every branch, forced glob refspecs, and refspecs `xargs` supplies. `--force-with-lease` and `-f` to another branch pass. `tests/test_force_push.py`, 22 tests on real git with config set by `git config` as a person would, refuses each form through `pushurl`, `insteadOf`, `pushInsteadOf`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, `push.default=upstream`, two `pushurl` values, `push.default=matching` from a feature branch, a configured `remote.*.push` and a configured mirror, and a `gh repo set-default`, `git remote`, `git branch -u`, tracking `switch`, `checkout` or `worktree add` earlier in the same line.
- **Verified, criterion 65 for this guard:** a program word that is a variable, text piped into a shell, `eval` of a variable, a `!` alias, an alias whose aliases were not read, and `xargs git` are refused; `cd "$D" && make` and the heredoc commit and pull-request idioms pass. A push of a substituted branch name is refused with a message pointing to `git push -u origin HEAD`.
- **Verified, criterion 55's alias that force-pushes main:** the runner now expands a git alias before it works out a push's target, so a stored alias that force-pushes main is refused, and one that pushes a branch passes, in cases, in the real-git test and as a real call through the wiring.
- **Verified, criterion 36 as amended (Q11):** `push_lease`, 55 cases, 24 new. While another live session holds the lease, a push from a linked worktree of the main copy's branch, named as a branch or in reflog form, of every branch, of a mirror, written or configured, or of a glob refspec is refused, and so is a push of HEAD, or a bare push, after a `switch` or `checkout` earlier in the line. The worktree's own branch, a deletion, a free lease, the holder itself and a dead holder pass. The push target now carries `branch` and `main_branch`, read from each git folder's `HEAD`.
- **Verified, Q16 and Q17:** `git_folder_shell`, 91 cases, 23 new. A persistent `core.hooksPath` write is refused, and so are removing the `core` section and renaming a section onto a guarded name, a parser gap handled in `wslib/gitrules.py`. It reads git aliases, so `git cf remote.origin.url URL` with `cf = config` is refused, and a call that is not one of git's own commands is refused when `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `HOME` or an include is set on it, or its aliases could not be read.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (626 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 76 of 76, before-shell's median 100 ms and the `git push` row's 101 against 200; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. Of 17 mutations of the guards' logic, 15 failed at least one case; the 2 that did not change only which message a refusal gives. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `c3470fa`, on Linux, macOS and Windows; macOS 3.9 passed on a re-run, with the `git push` row at 163 ms.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the four guidance pages the pull request changed and found 15 defects in 4 records: 10 contradictions, 2 enforcement gaps, 2 triggers and 1 placement. Fourteen were fixed, two in part and four in code: removing the `core` section, a reflog form of the main copy's branch, and a switch earlier in the line now refuse, and the push guard's own refusal says to start a worktree from `origin/main`. One, an `INDEX.md` row, is queued for slice Z below. A fresh re-check found every fix held, and one gap the fixes left on all four pages: HEAD in reflog form and `@{-1}` passed. Fixed in code.
- **Inferred, a stated limit:** from a worktree, a commit of the main copy's branch named by its id, another branch the holder made, and a tag still pass `push_lease`, since nothing records which commits are the holder's. Decision 0005 and the README say so. The lease and discard guards' refusals still advise a worktree made from the main copy's HEAD, which holds the holder's commits; queued for the maintainer as Q20.
- **Two fix rounds,** the most the stop rule allows. On the worktree push: the review's findings, then the re-check's reflog forms. On CI's macOS timing: the `git push` row went over 200 ms, first on macOS 3.13, then on macOS 3.9, where main's own runs reach 191. A push's target now takes three git calls, and a push that names literal refspecs and forces nothing takes none. The next run's macOS 3.9 job was over on before-shell, the `git push` row and before-edit, which this slice does not touch; it passed on a re-run, and main's own Q18 commit had failed on macOS 3.13 timing and passed on a re-run the same way.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The guard judges every destination, a local one included,** and the default branch is the part's `"branch"` setting, `main` by default, as `direct_push` names it. Judging only remote hosts would let a redirect to a local path decide.
- **A push it cannot judge is refused only when config could matter:** with no refspec, or when the line forces, deletes or prunes. A push that names literal refspecs and forces nothing cannot be forced by config, so it passes without its target, which also keeps the `git push` row inside its budget.
- **A call earlier in the line refuses only a forcing push,** and in `push_lease` only a push of HEAD or a bare one, so `git checkout -b x && git push -u origin x` passes.
- **`core.hooksPath` is refused only as a persistent write.** A `-c` value lasts one call, as `--no-verify` does. Unsetting it is refused like any guarded key, and so is removing the `core` section, since this workspace sets the key to its own hooks folder.
- **`git_folder_shell` passes a `!` alias a person stored and a program named `git-NAME`,** since it is always on and either is like a script; the README names both as misses.
- **`target_key` moved into `wslib/gitrules.py`,** so a part needs no import of the engine's fact module; `wslib/shellfacts.py` uses the same function.
- **The README's list of git commands that change config unseen took every miss the reviews verified,** not only the two Q18 named, so the In short line's pointer to it holds.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5c.

### C5b, session-start notices

Pull request #38, on branch `m3/c5b` from main at `2e3ca7c`, merged as `738d6f9` by `python3 ws.py merge 38`, at the driver tier. No approval in the maintainer's list names C5b: the CI notice's approval was recorded by C5 on 2026-11-21, and the split's (Q12) by C5a. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 69:** `ci_notice`, a new session-start hook, 8 cases, and `wslib/ci.py`, which reads main's CI with `gh` through build and parse functions. `tests/test_session_notices.py` runs them on `gh` answers recorded from this repository, `tests/records/ci-gh.json`. The newest run had passed, so nothing is shown and no second call is made. With that run left out, the failed merge of #37 is shown: 1 run in a row, its start, its failing macOS 3.9 step and its link. Shapes built from the record, each named there: a streak of 3, a full list of 20 as "at least", a full list with a cancelled run in it, and runs from pull requests, unfinished, cancelled or skipped passed over. No `gh`, a failing `gh`, the time limit, an answer it cannot read, and an `origin` that is a local path or names no repository each show nothing; another host is asked and says nothing. Through the real runner, with a stand-in `gh` on the path and both tools' recorded session-start payloads, a red main is shown, a green one is not, and a `gh` that sleeps past a 1-second limit shows nothing and returns in time.
- **Verified, criterion 59:** `python3 ws.py handoff NOTE` writes a marker keyed by branch in the working copy's git folder, `--done` removes it, and with no argument it lists them; `handoff_loader`, a new session-start hook, 7 cases. On throwaway repositories: writing again updates the marker in place and leaves another branch's byte for byte. From both tools' recorded payloads, one pending marker shows its note, two show none and list both, and none shows nothing. A cleared marker is not shown, nor is one whose branch merged locally, on origin, or before it was deleted. A branch with no commits of its own, a note on main and a squash-merged branch stay pending. A worktree's marker shows only in that worktree; a long note is cut and says so; a missing note is named. An edit tool's write and a shell write to the marker folder are refused by `git_folder` and `git_folder_shell`, 2 new cases each, while `python3 ws.py handoff` passes. `tests/hooks/session-start.json` gains 3 real calls through both tools' wiring.
- **Verified, from both agent tools:** `tests/records/handoff-loader-runs.json` holds one recorded run each of Claude Code 2.1.330 and codex-cli 0.169.0, in a scratch copy with one pending marker. Each agent replied with the note's goal line and its branch. Codex left the copy's own hooks untrusted, as in the earlier records, so its session-start hook was the same launcher call, given on the command line.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (674 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 79 of 79, session-start's median 159 ms against 2,000 and before-shell's 94 against 200; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. A real `gh run list` here took 0.8 seconds. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `6022d6c`, on Linux, macOS and Windows; macOS 3.13 passed on a re-run.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the four guidance pages the slice changed and found 12 defects in 4 records: 7 contradictions, 2 triggers, 2 enforcement gaps and 1 step. Eleven were fixed, three in code: the streak count with a passed-over run, the origin read inside the deadline, and the loader's wording, which now says to resume only when the person asks. One, an `INDEX.md` row, is queued for slice Z below. A fresh re-check found every fix held, one in part, and four things the fixes left on three pages; all four were fixed.
- **Two fix rounds,** the most the stop rule allows, on the review: its findings, then the re-check's four. On CI's macOS timing, one: the first run's macOS 3.9 job put before-shell at 253 ms and the `git push` row at 243, so the runner now imports the CI and handoff modules only where they are used. The next run's macOS 3.13 job put the `git push` row at 209 and passed on a re-run, at 184. Main's own macOS runs that day were at 140 to 154, and the same commit's two macOS jobs differed by up to 54 ms on rows this slice does not touch (inferred: runner noise, not this slice).

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A branch has merged when its tip, at writing or now, has reached `origin/main` since the note was written,** or `main` with no remote-tracking ref. So a branch with no commits of its own, a note on main and a squash or rebase merge stay pending until `--done`. The page and the README say so.
- **A merged marker is not deleted,** since a session hook only reminds and writing never touches another branch's marker. It stays, not shown, until `--done` or a new note on that branch; each start asks git again.
- **The notice says to resume only when the person asks,** after the review found it told every session to resume: a session starts before anyone says what it is for. Otherwise it says to mention the note once.
- **The loader reads only the session's own working copy,** as the row's "in the working copy's git folder" implies. A note is shown up to 8,000 characters, then cut with a pointer to the file.
- **A marker file is named by a hash of its branch,** so any branch name is a safe file name on every system.
- **The CI fact reads 20 runs of `ci.yml` on `main`, both set in the manifest.** It counts failure, timed-out and startup failure as failed and success as passed, and passes over the rest and every run from a pull request.
- **`gh` runs in a thread beside the fast-forward's fetch,** under one deadline of 3 seconds for the origin read and both calls. The real `gh run list` took 0.8 seconds; a second call is made only when main is red.
- **An `origin` on another host is asked,** since GitHub hosts other than github.com exist; gh's failure there says nothing.
- **The recorded tool runs were made twice,** as the plan's limits allow for C5's recorded runs, since the review changed the loader's wording.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5d.

Not done: nothing
Parked: none
Next: coordinator — build slice C5e, then C5f, as the maintainer's order sets; slice Z adds the `INDEX.md` rows queued below.

### TFIX, the hook timing fix

Pull request #39, on branch `m3/tfix` from main at `1fa880a`, at the driver tier, following the fix-a-bug playbook. The approval (Q21, and the maintainer's later word that the fix be general) is recorded under "Changes after approval". This entry is committed on the branch, and the branch merges by `python3 ws.py merge 39` once CI passes on its head. It touches no file the eval hashes.

- **Verified, the cause:** a profile of one `git push` hook call in a scratch copy, on the maintainer's Mac with Python 3.9, spent 38 of 68 ms running the cases of the six before-shell hooks: 463 cases on every call, a number each guard slice grows. Imports took 17 ms, 5 of them `runpy` alone. A push from a linked worktree asked git the same `rev-parse` question of the same folder three times, and each edit's git config fact made two git calls one after the other.
- **Verified, the fix:** a hook call now skips a hook's cases when they passed before on exactly the same files. `wslib/parts.py` keeps the record, `ws-cases.json`, in the working copy's own git folder: a digest of the part, its cases, every engine and workspace module loaded when they passed, and the Python and system. Any change to one of those runs the cases again, and a failure is never recorded. Every other command, the commit check and CI included, still runs every case every time. Within one hook call, each folder's git folders are asked of git once. The git config fact's two calls run at once. The launcher runs a script without `runpy`, and the runner imports `baseline`, `blocks`, `gate`, `merge` and `pins` only in the commands that use them.
- **Verified, test first:** `CaseMemoryTests` in `tests/test_core.py` (9 tests) failed before the change and pass after. They show a second call runs no case, an edit to the part, its cases, a module the cases reached through a lazy import, or the Python runs them again, a failing part is refused on every call, a forged entry beside changed content is not trusted, and a check command, a missing git folder or an unreadable record runs them every time. `OneCallTests` in `tests/test_shellfacts.py` (3 tests) show three lookups of one folder ask git once inside a hook call and three times outside one, and a timeout stays a timeout.
- **Verified, criterion 28 in CI:** two runs on the pull request, every job green. macOS with Python 3.9: before-shell 107 and 140 ms, before-edit 104 and 133, the `git push` row 120 and 150, the `gh write` row 107 and 122. macOS with Python 3.13: before-shell 115 and 119, before-edit 132 and 122, `git push` 123 and 136, `gh write` 91 and 95. In the seven failing macOS jobs on main and C5b and C5d before the fix, those rows ran 145 to 253 ms. The budgets and the way the runner times a call are unchanged.
- **Verified, locally:** an interleaved comparison with main's tree, three full runs of `tests/hook_cases.py` each, gave medians of 92 against 59 ms for before-shell, 99 against 71 for the `git push` row, 78 against 50 for `gh write` and 75 against 61 for before-edit. `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (686 tests, 3 skipped) pass; `evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference.
- **Verified, impact check:** `load_hooks` has four callers in `wslib/runner.py`, the session, after-edit, stop and tool-call paths, and all four pass the runner's tests and the hook case runner. `load` keeps its signature; `load_all`, `ws.py selftest`, `test_cases_bite` and the session-start findings fact call it without a record. `wslib/start.py` starts four scripts in the wiring, CI and the README: `ws.py`, `tests/hook_cases.py`, `evals/grade.py` and `tools/setup_steps.py`; each ran through the launcher. `-m` still runs through `runpy`. `_run` in `wslib/shellfacts.py` keeps its answers, now through `_start` and `_finish`.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed `docs/parts.md` and `docs/glossary.md`, the two guidance pages the slice changed, and found 4 contradictions in 2 records. All four were fixed in one round: the glossary's Case row and the In short line still said the cases run every time, the paragraph gave tool calls alone as the reason, and the glossary's first fix covered checks as well as hooks.
- **Inferred:** the first hook call after a change to a part or the engine pays the full cost, as every call did before. The runner's five repeats per case include that first call, so a fifth of its samples are such calls.
- **Inferred:** anyone who can write the git folder and compute a digest could make a broken hook skip its cases. That needs a write the `git_folder` and `git_folder_shell` guards refuse, and is no easier than editing the cases file itself.
- **Verified:** Windows still only reports, as criterion 28 allows; its `git push` row ran 202 and 218 ms in the second run.
- One fix round, on the review's findings. Main moved during the slice, so the branch merged main once; the plan's "Changes after approval" was the one conflict. No private ledger row maps to TFIX, which has no plan row, so none moved. No question was queued: the maintainer answered the scope during the slice.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A cache of passing cases, not a cheaper parser or a looser budget.** The cases were over half of a call and grow with every guard. `wslib/shell.py` is hashed by the eval, so it cannot change before slice Z, and the budget is criterion 28's. The record is keyed by content, not by time or commit, so it cannot go stale, and only hook calls read it.
- **The modules in the digest are those loaded when the cases passed,** so a lazy import inside `decide` counts. Over-including the runner's own modules costs only a rerun after an engine edit.
- **The record lives in the working copy's own git folder, not the common one,** so a worktree and the main copy holding different engines do not overwrite each other's entries.
- **The per-call memo of git folders lasts one hook call only.** A hook decides before its command runs, so nothing it asks about changes meanwhile; a longer-lived memo would go stale in tests and in a long process.

### C5e, the outward-write guard

Pull request #40, on branch `m3/c5e` from main at `0d7c926`, merged as `393459e` by `python3 ws.py merge 40`, which skipped the fast-forward because another session held the main working copy, at the driver tier. Main moved during the slice, so the branch merged main once; the plan and the review index were the two conflicts, and both sides were kept. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 39:** `outward_write`, a new before-shell hook, 271 cases, and `outward_list`, a new before-edit hook, 7 cases. The list is `outward-repos.json`, named in both manifest entries and listing only this repository. Outside the list, each read subcommand in the part's `READS` is allowed, a case each, and so is `gh api` with GET; `gh pr create`, `comment`, `review`, `ready`, `edit`, `close` and `merge`, `gh issue create`, `comment`, `edit` and `close`, `gh release create`, `gh repo fork` and `gh label create` are refused, and pass on the listed repository. `gh api` with `-X` POST, PATCH, PUT or DELETE, with `-f`, `-F`, `--raw-field` or `--field` and no method, or with `--input`, is refused, and so is a write whose path names no repository and `gh gist create`. `-R`, `--repo`, gh's set-default value and `GH_REPO=<listed> gh api repos/<other>/… -f …` are read as gh reads them. Two remotes and no gh default refuse a write. `git push` to an unlisted URL is refused; to a local path or a `file://` URL it passes; an scp-style `host:path` is not a local path. On every repository, `gh pr review --approve` in each form, a reviews call with `event` APPROVE, a variable event, `--input` or an `@file` field, and every `gh api graphql` call, a plain query included, are refused. An edit tool's write to the list is refused. `tests/test_outward.py`, 18 tests on real git with config set as a person would, shows the same through `pushurl`, `insteadOf`, `pushInsteadOf`, `remote.pushDefault`, `branch.*.pushRemote`, a stored alias, gh's set-default value and `GH_REPO`.
- **Verified, criterion 65 for this guard:** a program word that is a variable, text piped into a shell, `eval` of a variable, a gh subcommand that is a variable or is not one of gh's own, arguments `xargs` or `find -exec` give gh or `git push`, a git subcommand that is a variable, a `!` alias and an alias whose aliases were not read are refused; `cd "$D" && make` and the heredoc commit and pull-request idioms pass, as cases and as real calls.
- **Verified, criterion 55's parts:** a redirect, `tee`, `sed -i`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm` and `unlink` onto the list are refused, and so are `git mv`, `rm`, `checkout` and `restore` naming it; a stored alias that pushes to an unlisted repository is refused and one that pushes here passes; `git remote add` and `set-url` to an unlisted URL are refused, in cases, real-git tests and real calls through the wiring.
- **Verified, Q20:** the `lease` and `no_discard` refusals advise `git worktree add --no-track -b BRANCH .claude/worktrees/NAME origin/main`, as `push_lease`'s does; `tests/test_core.py` and a real call each for the two hooks assert the command.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (704 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 88 of 88, before-shell's median 64 ms, the `git push` row's 77 and the `gh write` row's 57 against 200; `tests.test_cases_bite` passes; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. Of 19 mutations of `outward_write`'s logic, each failed at least one case. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `106f924`, on Linux, macOS and Windows: macOS 3.9 put before-shell at 118 ms, the `git push` row at 122 and `gh write` at 126; macOS 3.13 at 118, 125 and 119; Windows reports 287 on the `git push` row, as criterion 28 allows.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the four guidance pages the slice changed and found 12 defects in 4 records: 5 enforcement, 5 contradictions, 1 step and 1 placement. All 12 were fixed, one in part, five of them in code: `git send-pack` and `git http-push` are judged by their URL, `git mv`, `rm`, `checkout` and `restore` of the list are refused, a push recursing into submodules and a push after `gh pr checkout` in the same line are refused, a typed URL gets `pushInsteadOf` before `insteadOf`, and the list matches a URL by its whole path. A fresh re-check found all 12 held, three in part, and 8 enforcement defects the fixes left, across the 4 records; all were fixed in the second round. The records carry no severity marks: they were written before B3's severity mark reached this branch.
- **Two fix rounds,** the most the stop rule allows: the review's findings, then the re-check's. CI passed on its first run of the final head.
- **Inferred, stated limits:** the guard reads command lines, so a script, an interpreter, `curl`, a shell alias, a program named `git-NAME` and the web page get past it, and so does PowerShell under Codex on Windows. A `push.recurseSubmodules` or `submodule.recurse` a person set in config is not read. A git command that rewrites the list without naming it, such as `git checkout other -- .`, `git apply` or a merge, is not seen. On a listed repository every gh write passes, `gh repo delete` among them. The README's limits say each.
- **Inferred:** this session wrote the list's first version before the guard existed, as the plan asks: only this repository is listed. From the guard's merge on, an agent's change to it is refused in the cases above.
- **Staging:** the second fix round's commit was staged with `git add -u` in this slice's own worktree, against the contract's staging rule; `git status` showed only this slice's files, and each was then recorded by name in the ledger.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The list is its own fact, read by the runner,** not a file the part opens: decision 0004 holds a part to its event and config, so `repositories` joins the before-shell needs.
- **A push's URLs come from a fact of their own,** `destinations`, in one git call for a push that names a remote, since the full `targets` fact takes three and `outward_write` must judge every push, the common `git push -u origin HEAD` included. A test shows the two facts give the same URLs.
- **The list is matched by host and whole path,** each entry `HOST/PATH`, so a GitLab subgroup or an Azure Repos project is told apart, after the review found that the last two segments let another group's project through.
- **The list's file is matched by its name in any folder,** so a worktree's copy and a folder the parser cannot place are covered; a different file of the same name elsewhere is refused too.
- **gh writes that create a repository or act on an account** (`gh repo create` and `fork`, `gh project`, org or user secrets and variables) are refused everywhere, since no listed repository holds them; `gh issue transfer` is judged by the repository it moves the issue to.
- **A gh command that is not gh's own is refused,** since a gh alias or extension may run anything, and so are `gh alias set` and `gh extension install`, which name no repository.
- **The approval of a workflow run or a deployment counts as an approval,** refused on every repository with a pull request's.
- **`pushInsteadOf` applies to a typed URL,** as real git showed; C3's test that expected `insteadOf` alone now expects git's answer, and `no_force_push`'s target is corrected with it.
- **A remote word that names no remote, and a folder that does not exist yet, leave a push unresolved,** after the re-check found `git clone URL t && git -C t push` read `origin` as a local path.
- **`submodule.recurse` is refused as a persistent `git config` write** by `git_folder_shell`, beside `core.hooksPath`, outside the eval's hash until slice Z.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5b.
