### C5f, secrets

Pull request #41, on branch `m3/c5f` from main at `d8c5c77`, merged as `da5a0d8` by `python3 ws.py merge 41`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's approvals page names no approval for C5f, so none was recorded before building. No private ledger row maps to C5f, so none moved (verified, a search of the ledger). No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 40, the dumps:** `secrets_shell`, a new before-shell hook, 138 cases. `env` with no program, `printenv` in every form, `export`, `declare` and `typeset` with no name, `set` with no argument, `ps` with `e` or `E` in a letter group or with `-E`, a read of `/proc/<pid>/environ`, and PowerShell's `env:` drive and its environment-variables method are refused. `env FOO=1 make`, `export NAME=value`, `declare -p NAME`, `set -euo pipefail`, `ps -ef` and `ps aux` pass, a case each.
- **Verified, criterion 40, the reads:** a command that names a secrets file is refused, in an argument or after `<`. The files are the manifest's `"secrets"` and `"home"` patterns, which a test holds equal to `agent_settings`'s, and the file `with-secrets` reads. Cases cover `cat`, `source`, `.`, `grep`, `sed`, `awk`, `cp`, `find`, `git show HEAD:.env`, `git add`, `git commit -F`, `--body-file=`, `--env-file=`, `@.env`, an interpreter's `-c` text, paths by `~`, `$HOME`, an absolute path and a quoted Windows path, `< .env` in a loop, in a substitution, in `eval` and in `sh -c`, `bash -lc`, `-c -e` and `-c -o pipefail`, a variable the line sets, and globs such as `.e*` and `cat *`. `ls`, `test`, `rm`, `git status`, `git check-ignore`, `git rm --cached`, `grep` for the text `.env`, a commit message or pull-request body naming `.env`, the heredoc commit idiom and `cd "$D" && make` pass.
- **Verified, criterion 40, `ws.py with-secrets`:** `tests/test_secrets.py`, 16 tests, runs the command for real in throwaway repositories with a fake secrets file. The child sees the named value and no other; from `sh -c`, the calling shell prints the variable unset after the child printed it. `sh`, `bash`, `/bin/sh`, `zsh`, `pwsh`, `CMD.EXE`, `powershell.exe`, `env`, `/usr/bin/env`, `printenv`, `xargs`, `sudo` and `nohup` as the child exit 2 with no value printed, and so does a symbolic link to a shell under another name. A missing name, file or `--` exits 2 with no value. The child's exit code comes back. A linked worktree reads the main copy's file, and `WS_SECRETS_FILE` moves it. The hook refuses every child the command refuses (a test over the shared list), and git, gh, a write, a wrapper, a dump and a nested `with-secrets` under it (cases).
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (720 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 93 of 93, before-shell's median 64 ms against 200; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `a874d2c`, on Linux, macOS and Windows: macOS 3.9 put before-shell at 130 ms, the `git push` row at 148 and `gh write` at 157; macOS 3.13 at 100, 108 and 98; Windows reports 258 to 318, as criterion 28 allows. The first run, on `2626887`, failed every check job: the access test read decision 0004 before its row was committed, as in C5c.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** two fresh driver-tier helpers reviewed the four guidance pages the slice changed and found 14 defects in 4 records: 7 enforcement, 6 contradictions and 1 trigger, 2 of them blocking. The two blocking findings had one cause: Codex's opt-in profile denies `.env` to every process, `with-secrets` included, and the pages did not say so. All 14 were fixed, one in part, six of them in code: `ps eww` is refused, a shell's `-lc` and `-c -e` text is read, a nested `with-secrets` is refused, the configured file is refused to the shell, a Windows batch file with an argument cmd.exe reads is refused, and the program list moved to `wslib/secretrules.py`, which reads no file, so the part no longer imports the module that does. A fresh re-check found 11 held and 3 in part, and 5 new defects, 1 blocking: the pages told a person to change `workspace.json`'s `"file"`, which a test pinned to `.env` and which every person shares. The second round fixed all 5: `WS_SECRETS_FILE`, set in a person's own shell, moves the file, and the hook refuses an agent's command that sets it; an absolute `"file"`, `ps Eww`, `bash -c -o pipefail` and `find` or `parallel` under `with-secrets` are refused.
- **Two fix rounds,** the most the stop rule allows: the review's findings, then the re-check's. CI passed on its first run of the final head.
- **Verified, a defect found after the merge:** the hook refuses any command whose text holds the name of PowerShell's environment-variables method, even inside a heredoc or a quoted message, since it matches that name on the raw line: writing this entry through a shell heredoc was refused. It refuses too much, never too little, and its message still says only to ask the person. It is not fixed here: the slice has used its two fix rounds. A fix matches the name only where the parser reads no command, and gives the refusal the dump message's advice.
- **Inferred, stated limits:** the hook reads command lines, so an interpreter or a script that opens a secrets file, a recursive read such as `grep -r` or `tar`, files `xargs` or `find -exec` supply, a variable the line does not set, brace expansion, an unquoted Windows path, PowerShell beyond its `env:` drive, and an environment dump by an interpreter or `awk` get past it; `echo "$TOKEN"` prints what the shell holds. Under `with-secrets`, a child that prints its environment, an interpreter told to, or `make`, `npm run` or `npx` can still show the value. The README and agent settings say each.
- **Inferred:** that cmd.exe runs a special character in a batch file's arguments comes from Python's documentation; no run on Windows showed it. That Codex's profile lets `with-secrets` read a file under `~/.config/` rests on agent settings' statement that the profile denies nothing in the home folder. That `ps Eww` prints the environment on macOS comes from its manual.
- **Staging:** a shell variable once passed the whole file list to `ws.py ledger --add` as one path, so the slice's worktree ledger holds one entry that names no file. The worktree is removed with the slice, and the entry with it.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A read is any command that names a secrets file,** unless its program only looks at the name: `ls`, `stat`, `test`, `rm`, `touch`, `echo`, `git status`, `git check-ignore`, `git ls-files`, `git rm` and their kind. Listing readers instead would miss every program not on the list. The cost is that some harmless commands are refused, such as `find . -name .env` and `cat *`; the refusal says to name the files.
- **`printenv` is refused in every form,** since `printenv NAME` prints a token as surely as a dump does. The refusal says to echo by name a variable that holds no secret.
- **A write to a secrets file by a redirection passes;** the row asks for reads. A program that also reads, such as `cp` or `tee`, is refused.
- **A variable the line does not set passes,** as `git_folder_shell` judges a write; refusing every `cat "$F"` would refuse ordinary work.
- **The values file is `.env` at the main working copy's top,** so every worktree reads one file, named by the manifest's `"file"`. A person whose sandbox denies it sets `WS_SECRETS_FILE` in their own shell, as `WS_DENY_LIST` moves the deny list.
- **`with-secrets` starts its program with no shell of its own** and refuses shells, `env`, `printenv` and programs that run another, by the name given and by the file a link leads to.
- **The hook refuses git, gh and writes under `with-secrets`,** since the other shell guards read only Python there. Slice Z's parser change, queued below, lets every guard judge the program instead.
- **The parser's missing input redirections are read in the part,** from `wslib.shell.parse`, outside the eval's hash, until slice Z.
- **No new recorded tool run.** The hook is a before-shell part like C5c's and C5e's; the hook case runner calls it through both tools' wiring, a real call each for Claude Code and Codex.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5e.

### C6, not inherited

Pull request #42, on branch `m3/c6` from main at `71ee12b`, merged as `c96a5c3` by `python3 ws.py merge 42`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's approvals page names no approval for C6, so none was recorded before building. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, no guard needed a fix:** before any change, a scratch run called each guard's refusing hook case through the real wiring once with every variable set and once with each of 22 removed. Every call still refused. With `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` unset, Claude Code's wiring cannot find the launcher and `|| exit 2` refuses. With `CLAUDE_PID` unset, the lease refuses because the session has no process id. The commit hook, run with each variable removed, still refused. Both weaknesses the catalog names for the source workspace were already absent here, so this slice adds the proof, not a fix.
- **Verified, criterion 46, the variables:** `tests/test_not_inherited.py`, 12 tests. `GUARDS` lists each hook that refuses a tool call, 12 in all, each with a refusing case from `tests/hooks/`; a test holds the list equal to the manifest's refusing before-event hooks. `CODEX` adds Codex's wiring, one guard per event. `READERS` adds a second call for `git_folder_shell` and `outward_write`, where they read `HOME` and `GH_REPO`. `GIT_HOOKS` runs the pre-commit, commit-msg and pre-push hooks on a scratch copy whose staged page holds a made-up private term and a key-shaped string, outside the edit ledger. Each call runs with every variable in `VARIABLES` set to a working value, then with each one removed: it must still refuse, with the same findings or for a reason `FAILS_CLOSED` names. A scan of `ws.py`, `wslib/`, `parts/`, the git hooks, the launcher and both wirings fails on a variable read by name that `VARIABLES` does not list.
- **Verified, criterion 46, the opt-out:** with `WS_DENY_LIST` unset and no list at the default path, the commit check warns and checks nothing; with the list at the default path it still finds the term; set to a missing file, it is an error. The README's checks table names this as the one intended opt-out.
- **Verified, criterion 46, the kill:** the pre-commit hook, killed with its whole process tree at seven points from 5 to 90 percent of a warm run, leaves the working copy, its git folder, the temporary folder and the worktree list exactly as they were. The commit check reads the staged files in memory and writes no file of its own.
- **Verified, the test bites:** four planted defects each made it fail: a hook that allows when `CLAUDE_PID` is unset, an unlisted marker variable that turns hooks off, the deny list opting out whenever its variable is unset, and a temporary file the commit check holds while it runs.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` pass locally (731 tests, 3 skipped, before the review's fix rounds added one; CI ran 732); the new module takes 68 seconds on macOS. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `5e6cb2c`, on Linux, macOS and Windows. The Windows 3.13 job took 18 minutes, against about 11 before the slice. The first run, on `4d7261b`, failed on Linux, Windows and the Linux cold clone: the test points git's global config at an empty file, so the message check found no identity, where macOS makes one up. The fix puts an identity in the test's home.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** a fresh driver-tier session reviewed the README change and found 5 defects, none blocking: 1 contradiction, 3 enforcement and 1 placement. The stop gate also turns off when `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset, which the row's "one opt-out" missed; the test reached no variable-reading code in `git_folder_shell` and `outward_write`; the variable list was hand-picked but read as complete; a pin move's fetch was untested; and the kill rule hid inside the variable row. All 5 were fixed, one in the test. A fresh re-check found 4 held and 1 held in part, and 2 new defects, neither blocking: the Limits bullet claimed a second call for every guard that reads a variable, which `git_folder` lacks, and counted the push check's tutorial clone among the commit check's exceptions. The second round fixed both, in text.
- **Two fix rounds** on the review, the most the stop rule allows; the CI failure took one fix of its own, on a different failure.
- **Inferred, stated limits:** the README says each. The variables git, gh, Python and the shell read are chosen by hand, so one left out, such as `PYTHONPATH`, is not tested, and two removed together are not. A variable that names a protected file, such as `HOME` or `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` for git's global config, changes which file that is when removed. The stop gate is not covered: its wiring lets a session stop when the launcher fails, by C1's design. A first run after an engine change killed while Python compiles may leave a half-written file in `__pycache__`. A commit that moves a pin fetches in the pinned repository, and a kill while git updates a ref there may leave git's lock; the test moves no pin. A push check killed while it runs a tutorial leaves the tutorial's clone in the temporary folder.
- **Inferred, a side finding outside this row:** the before-shell reminder that opens the edit ledger runs `git status` with git's optional index lock, so a hard kill of that hook in that moment could leave a stale `index.lock`, which `git_folder_shell` then refuses an agent to remove.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A guard is a hook that refuses a tool call, plus the commit, message and push hooks.** The stop gate and the session hooks are left out, as C1's row says a guard's wiring refuses and the stop gate's passes.
- **A refusal for another reason counts as working.** A guard that fails closed has not turned off; `FAILS_CLOSED` names each such variable and why, so a new one fails the test until it is named.
- **The variable list is the code's reads plus the programs' own,** checked by a scan, instead of every variable in the runner's environment: CI holds about a hundred, and the Windows job already grew by about seven minutes.
- **The kill uses SIGKILL on the whole process group,** or `taskkill /T /F` on Windows, the hardest case: nothing the check could do on a gentler signal runs.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C5f.

Proposed revision, as template rule 7 asks: the C6 row and criterion 46 say "no guard"; the README and the test say "no guard on a tool call, a commit or a push", since the stop gate's wiring passes when the launcher fails. The maintainer may reword the row and the criterion to match, or ask for the stop gate to fail closed, which would undo C1's choice.

### PSFIX, the PowerShell method's name

Pull request #43, on branch `m3/psfix` from main at `8c4ccc7`, at the driver tier, following the fix-a-bug playbook. The approval, and the maintainer's approval of C6's proposed revision of criterion 46, are recorded under "Changes after approval" as two entries of 2026-11-23, committed on the branch before the fix. This entry is committed on the branch, and the branch merges by `python3 ws.py merge 43` once CI passes on its head. It touches no file the eval hashes and no guidance page.

```
Fixed: secrets_shell refused any command whose raw text held the name of PowerShell's environment-variables method, even in a heredoc or a commit message; it now reads the name only where a command can run it.
```

- **Verified, the bug, through the real wiring:** on main's tree, Claude Code's before-shell wiring refused `cat >> FILE <<'EOF'` with the name in the heredoc's text, and `git commit -m` with the name in the message, both with exit 2. On the branch's tree the same two calls pass, and `[Environment]::` with the method, piped to `Out-String`, is still refused, now with the dump message's advice.
- **Verified, test first:** 20 new cases in `parts/secrets_shell.cases.json`. Before the fix, `ws.py selftest` failed 7 of 158: the name in a heredoc that writes a file, quoted or not, in a commit message, in a pull request body, in the heredoc commit idiom, in a heredoc git reads with `-F -`, and in a heredoc Python reads. The 13 refusals the fix must keep already held: the method followed by another command, piped on, in lower case, with an argument, inside parentheses, assigned to a variable, under `pwsh -c`, `powershell.exe -Command`, `cmd /c` and `sh -c`, in a heredoc fed to `pwsh`, and in a substitution inside a commit message or an unquoted heredoc. A new unit test, `test_the_powershell_method_gets_the_dump_advice` in `tests/test_secrets.py`, holds the refusal to the dump message's advice. After the fix, 158 of 158 pass.
- **Verified, the fix:** `decide` refuses the name when it is in the program word or any argument of a command the parser reads, message bodies left out, and in what remains of the raw line once message bodies, and heredoc bodies fed to a program that is not a shell, are taken out. The raw line is still read because the parser reads PowerShell's `[Environment]::Method()` as no command at all. The parse runs only when the line holds the name, so other commands cost nothing more. The refusal says to echo by name a variable that holds no secret and to ask the person for a secret, as the dump message does.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (733 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 93 of 93, before-shell's median 69 ms against 200; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs on `181151a`, on Linux, macOS and Windows. macOS 3.9 put before-shell at 134 ms, the `git push` row at 145 and `gh write` at 150; macOS 3.13 at 171, 177 and 178.
- **Inferred:** the macOS 3.13 rows are runner noise, not the fix. The new code runs only on a line that holds the name, which no timed case does, and main's last runs put the same rows anywhere from 79 to 151 ms.
- **Verified, impact check:** `decide` is called by the runner's before-shell path, by `ws.py selftest` and the hook case runner through both tools' wiring (`tests/hooks/before-shell.json`, five secrets rows, all passing), by `tests/test_secrets.py` and by `tests/test_not_inherited.py`, which uses the secrets-file row; all pass. The fix reads `shell.parse` and `secretrules.SHELLS` and changes neither. `_child_refusal` and the file and dump checks are unchanged.
- **Inferred, stated limits:** the name is still refused where it is a plain word of a command that does not run it, such as `echo` or a `grep` pattern for the name, and in a message body whose quoting the line spells differently from the parsed text, such as escaped quotes inside double quotes. Both refuse too much, never too little. A heredoc fed to any shell keeps its text, so the name in a `bash` heredoc's text is refused.
- No fix round: the cases passed on the first run of the fix, and CI on its first run. Main moved twice during CI, so the branch merged main twice; the milestone record's table and the plan's "Changes after approval" were the conflicts. No instruction review: the slice changed no guidance page. No private ledger row maps to PSFIX, which has no plan row, so none moved (verified, a search of the ledger; its check prints 0 unmapped). No question was queued.
- **Verified, a side finding, queued for slice Z below:** `docs/agent-settings.md`'s dump bullet and the `secrets_shell` row of `docs/parts.md` name PowerShell's `env:` drive but not the method, which C5f's hook already refused.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **Read the name in a command's words and in the raw line's remainder, not in the words alone.** The parser reads `[Environment]::Method()` as a function definition and reports no command, so a words-only match would have let the plain PowerShell form through. Taking the text bodies out of the raw line keeps every refusal the old match made, except the ones in text no shell runs.
- **A heredoc fed to a shell keeps its text,** PowerShell and `cmd` among them, since the parser reads neither's script. That refuses `bash <<EOF` text that names the method, which is rare and harmless.
- **No change to the guidance pages.** The method was missing from them before this slice, and adding it needs an instruction review; it is queued for slice Z, which edits those pages anyway.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C6.

Not done: nothing
Parked: none

### KILLFIX, the kill test's timing

Pull request #45, on branch `m3/killfix` from main at `713fe88`, at the driver tier, following the fix-a-bug playbook. The maintainer's approval is recorded under "Changes after approval" as an entry of 2026-11-23, committed on the branch before the fix. This entry is committed on the branch, and the branch merges by `python3 ws.py merge 45` once CI passes on its head. It touches no file the eval hashes and no guidance page.

```
Fixed: C6's kill test placed its kill points by one timed run of the commit check, so a slow sample put a point after the check had ended; it now measures the run time and never kills a check that has already ended.
```

- **Verified, the failure on main:** attempt 1 of CI run 45174839367, on `df370a8`, failed only the macOS job with Python 3.9, at point 0.6: "the check ended before it was killed, so this kill tested nothing". The rerun passed.
- **Verified, the cause, reproduced before the fix:** a scratch copy of the pre-commit hook that sleeps one second on its second run, the one main's test times, made main's test fail at point 0.6 with the same message. The test timed one whole run and placed every point as a fraction of that one sample.
- **Verified, the fix:** `tests/test_not_inherited.py` now takes the run time as the shortest of three whole runs, each started exactly as a killed run is. `kill_inside` polls the run up to its kill point. A run that ends first is timed, its time becomes the new shortest, and the point is tried again on a new run, up to three times. Every point, not only those up to 0.6, must now kill a running check. The checks of the working copy, the git folder, the temporary folder and the worktree list are unchanged.
- **Verified, the fix holds under a slow sample:** with the same scratch hook, the fixed test passes with the second run slowed, and with all three measured runs slowed, where it took one retry. Ten plain runs in a row passed, and five under eight busy processes.
- **Verified, test first for the helper:** two new unit tests in `KillInsideTests`. A run of 0.4 seconds measured as 3 is killed while running on the second try, and a long run is killed on the first.
- **Verified, the test still bites:** a scratch hook that holds a lock file while the check runs, and removes it at the end, failed the test from the first point.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (735 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 93 of 93; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference. CI passed all five check jobs on `2565c8e`, on Linux, macOS and Windows.
- **Verified, impact check:** `kill_inside`, `start_check`, `run_time` and `sleeper` are new and used only in this test file. `kill_tree` and `listing` are unchanged, and only this file calls them.
- **Inferred, stated limit:** the test can still fail if three runs in a row each end before their point, each faster than the one before. That needs the check to speed up sharply within one test, and it would then say so, with the three run times.
- No fix round. Main moved during the slice, so the branch merged main once; the plan's "Changes after approval" was the one conflict. No instruction review: the slice changed no guidance page. No private ledger row maps to KILLFIX, which has no plan row, so none moved (verified, a search of the ledger; its check prints 0 unmapped). No question was queued.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **Measure and correct, not widen the margin.** Moving the asserted points lower, or timing more runs, would only make the flake rarer. Timing the run that ended early gives a real run time, so the next try's point falls inside the run.
- **Every point must now kill a running check.** The old test let points 0.75 and 0.9 test nothing in silence. With retries, all seven points are real kills.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to PSFIX.

Not done: nothing
Parked: none
Next: coordinator — merge pull request #45 once CI passes on its head, then build slice C8, the next slice of lane C.

- **Verified, criterion 47, drift:** a hand edit to a synced file makes `ws.py drift` exit 1 and name the file, and `ws.py check` fails it through the new `kit_drift` check; an edit to a seeded file does not. `kit_drift` has 9 cases: deleted, edited, seeded, kept, line endings, a byte-order mark, an unreadable lock, and no lock at all. `drift --keep` makes a synced file the instance's own, and refuses a page the kit marks proven.
- **Verified, criterion 47, upgrade:** `UpgradeTests` makes newer kits from copies of this repository. An upgrade replaces an unedited synced file, adds a new one, deletes one the kit no longer ships or now classes never synced, leaves a kept file alone, names a new part, and sets the manifest's version. It refuses on drift, and on a new synced file at a path the instance already uses, and then no file in the instance changes.
- **Verified, criterion 47, the proof checks in an instance:** `proof` takes a synced page's marks from the lock when they equal it, fails them when they differ, and holds a page of the instance's own to every rule; `eval_record` does not gate a page the kit proved; `rules` accepts an `eval:` tag naming a kit case. Each has a case: four in `proof`, three in `eval_record`, one in `rules`. The adopted instance's `check` passes with fix a bug proven by the lock.
- **Verified, criterion 47, the record:** decision record 0007 holds the classes, the kill rules and the credit; `DecisionRecordTests` holds the kill rules and the credit to it.
- **Verified, the kit manifest:** `python3 ws.py kit` lists every tracked file with its class and `sha256:` hash, so it names every seeded item, with each synced page's proof state and the kit's eval cases. `ManifestTests` fails a tracked file no rule classes, a rule that matches no file, and a proof state that disagrees with this README's table.
- **Verified, criterion 64's kit half:** the instance's `.gitignore`, seeded from this repository's, names `.claude/settings.local.json`, and `.githooks/post-checkout` is synced into it, executable (an adopted instance and its lock, read).
- **Verified, the kit's merge setting (the entry of 2026-11-21):** the seed README's step 7 carries the README's merge-settings step.
- **Verified, an instance can commit:** with the hooks on, an instance's first commit and an upgrade's commit pass with `Instructions-review: skipped: as the kit wrote it`, which `review_trailer` checks against the commit's lock; a commit of a page the instance changed under that reason is refused. An upgrade that changes a proof state commits with the README's row corrected under a review record of its own. Five `review_trailer` cases and two tests hold this.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check`, `check --commits origin/main..HEAD`, `check --phase ci --changed-from origin/main`, `check --history`, `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` (93 of 93) and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (763 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally. CI passed all five check jobs and both cold-clone jobs on `8ae8f5b`, on Linux, macOS and Windows, after the branch was rebased onto main once B2t merged. It was rebased three more times as B4's batches and results reached main, and `ws.py merge` merges only when CI has passed on the head; the cold-clone log shows the adopted instance's setup steps ending in `drift: none`. The new tests add about a minute to the Linux test step and four and a half to Windows with Python 3.9 (1,021 seconds against main's 751), since each adopts and checks a whole instance.
- **Verified, the instruction review:** fresh driver-tier sessions reviewed the 11 guidance pages the slice changed and found 42 defects, 12 blocking. All 12 were fixed in the first fix round. Of the 30 others, 25 were fixed, 3 index rows are queued for slice Z below, 1 is deferred to M5, and 1 was declined. A fresh re-check found one new blocking defect, that an upgrade changing a synced page's proof state could not be committed, and five non-blocking items: four fixed, one deferred to M5. The second fix round fixed the blocking one, and a narrow fresh re-check found the fix holds and three non-blocking items, all fixed. The instruction review playbook, changed in the fix round, and the seed README's new section on checks each got a review of their own, with three non-blocking findings each, all fixed. So did the change that seeds the feature layer walk, with two non-blocking findings, both fixed. That is two fix rounds on the commit failure, the stop rule's limit, and the second held.
- **Verified, the feature layer walk is seeded:** B4's first batch added `docs/playbooks/feature-layer-walk.md`, whose lists of layers each instance rewrites, so it is seeded, not synced, as the private port ledger's row for it says. An instance's README gets a row for it, as its own page, beside the kit's rows; `ManifestTests` holds the seed table to this README's.
- **Verified, a side finding:** after B2t merged, its tutorial's link to the README's checks table failed in an adopted instance, whose README had no such heading. The seed README gained that section. A page pushed straight to main that links a file or heading an instance lacks fails `tests/test_kit.py` on main's next CI run, not at the commit.
- **Inferred, stated limits:** the lock is a file the instance can edit; a changed hash in it hides drift and passes the kit's skip reason, and shows only in the pull request's diff, since the lock is machinery. The kill rules count only instances the maintainer knows of. Six synced pages still say an edit to the contract or index needs an eval run, which a new instance cannot make (deferred to M5, which sets up the first instance).
- No question was queued. The private ledger's two rows for C8, the kit decision and the adoption page, moved to their pages (verified, its check prints 0 unmapped).

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The manifest is built when an instance is made, not committed with hashes.** Every edit to a synced page would also change a committed manifest, and the parallel lanes would conflict on it at each merge. `python3 ws.py kit` prints it, and adopt and upgrade write it into the instance as its lock.
- **Drift is a check as well as a command.** As a command alone, an edit would surface only when someone ran it. `kit_drift` fails it at the commit and in CI, and `drift --keep` still lets an instance diverge on record, which is what the first kill rule counts.
- **The contract and the index are seeded, not left out.** An instance's checks need both, and the README's steps start from the sample's. The index loses the rows that name what stays here; the contract loses its eval-run rule, which a new instance cannot keep, and the instance's eval page says when to add it back.
- **The kit's tests and CI stay here; an instance gets a seeded CI of its own,** which runs `selftest`, `drift` and the checks on three systems. The tests read this workspace's plans and records, and CI here runs them for each kit version.
- **The decision records are synced,** since the synced pages link them; a later kit record can take an instance's own number, and the upgrade then refuses.
- **A page the kit proved cannot be kept,** since its proof rests on the kit's text and records.
- **The skip reason "as the kit wrote it" is checked against the lock,** so an instance's first commit and its upgrades pass the review check without a record for each of about seventy pages, and a page the instance changed still needs its review.
- **Tier projection and generated hook wiring were not taken,** as decision 0007's credit says.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to KILLFIX.

### C9, the two-session demonstration

Pull request #49, on branch `m3/c9` from main at `ac636b9`, at the driver tier. The maintainer's approvals page names C9 only in lane C's order, which the entry of 2026-11-23 under "Changes after approval" already records, so no entry was added. This entry is committed on the branch, and the branch merges by `python3 ws.py merge 49` once CI passes on its head. No file the eval hashes was edited, and no guidance page, so no instruction review was needed (verified: `eval_record` passes, and `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference).

- **Verified, criterion 48, the run:** two headless Claude Code 2.1.330 sessions, on the small model, were started by one script on the main working copy of a scratch clone of main at `ac636b9`. Both session-start hooks fired at the same recorded moment. The clone's remote was a local bare repository. Session A's edit took the lease. Session B's edit in the main copy, 1.7 seconds later, was refused by `lease`, which named A's session and the worktree command. B ran `git worktree add --no-track -b session-b-orders-note .claude/worktrees/session-b-orders-note origin/main`, edited there, committed, and pushed `HEAD:main`. That push was rejected because A had pushed first. B rebased in its worktree and pushed again. The scratch remote's main holds A's commit, then B's, and each commit holds only its own session's file. A's session end released the lease.
- **Verified, the records:** `tests/records/two-sessions.json` holds each session's tool calls and what it was shown, its final reply, every payload a capture hook saw with its time, and the scratch remote's main afterwards. Paths are redacted.
- **Verified, the replay test:** `tests/test_two_sessions.py`. Six tests read the record. Three replay its 13 edit and shell calls and both session ends through the real runner, in recorded order, in a scratch clone with a bare remote. A live process plays each session's Claude Code process, and the test does each call's git step, so the next call meets the same state. Every decision matches what the session was shown. Two negative controls: with the holder's process dead, the refused edit takes the lease over; and B's push, made from the main copy instead, is refused by `push_lease`. Marking the refused edit as allowed in the loaded record fails the replay, and so does marking an allowed one as refused (verified, both run).
- **Verified, nothing reached this repository's main:** the scratch clone's remote was the bare repository's local path, as the recorded `git remote -v` shows. After the run, `git ls-remote origin` here still gave main at `ac636b9`. A test checks that neither scratch commit exists in this repository.
- **Verified, a stated limit:** the scratch clone had no `core.hooksPath`. The guards refused this session's `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` there, and the README makes that a person's step. So the git hooks did not run in the demonstration: no commit check, no pre-push check, and no post-commit hook to clear the edit ledgers. The hooks the agent tool runs did: the lease, the shell guards, the session hooks and the stop gate. That neither commit holds the other's files is shown by the remote's commits, not by the commit check. A person can rerun it with the hooks installed.
- **Verified, a side finding:** Claude Code itself blocks a foreground `sleep 60`. Each session ran it in the background and waited, as that block's message says. The block is the tool's, not this workspace's.
- **Inferred:** the prompt's one-minute sleep makes the refusal certain rather than a race. Without it, the holder could commit and push before the other's first edit. The lease would then pass by state, as decision 0005 allows. The two prompts differ only in file, line and subject, so either session could have held the lease.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (772 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 93 of 93. The private ledger's check prints 0 unmapped.
- No fix round. The run cost about two cents of model use, 22 turns in all. One question was queued, Q44, on main's red CI: the scheduled run of 2026-11-23 failed only the `git push` row's timing on macOS with Python 3.9, 214 ms against 200, with all 93 cases passing. No private ledger row maps to C9, so none moved (verified, a search of the ledger).

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The small model,** as the earlier recorded runs used. It also tests whether the refusal's text alone gets the cheapest model to a worktree. It did, at the first try.
- **Permission prompts off in the sessions,** so a headless permission refusal could not stand in for a hook's. The hooks still ran, and the lease still refused.
- **No way around the missing git hooks.** Passing `-c core.hooksPath` in the prompts would change what the agents do, and writing the config another way is what the guard refuses.
- **The replay plays each session's process with a live process of its own,** since the recorded process ids are gone. Each call's git step is done by the test, keyed on the recorded command.
- **No CHANGELOG entry,** as slices C1 to C8.

Not done: nothing
Parked: Q44

### Sub-batch A, 2026-11-18

Built on `m3/a` in `.claude/worktrees/m3-a`, at the driver tier. It was never pushed on its own.

**A0, the harness.** Verified: the `eval_record` cases (46) cover a case's own arms, additions to a shared arm, baseline and one-time records, a proof-key flip leaving a record current, link text staling it, re-records and the scale rule; `tests/test_m3_a.py` covers criteria 1, 2, 4, 5, 56, 61 and 67; `tests/test_shell.py` (157 tests) covers the parser side of criteria 55 and 58; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` gave no difference on M2's 135 transcripts after the harness changes, and on R1's 135 after recording. Inferred: criterion 55's guard refusals (a redirect into `.git/config`, a stored alias that force-pushes, and the rest) are the guards' to make; A0 gives them the facts, and C3 and C5 hold the refusals with cases.

**A2, the rules register.** Verified: `python3 ws.py rules` prints 36 rules: part 30, prose 10, eval 3 tags. `loaded_size` holds the contract and pointers to 6,000 bytes (4,462 now) and the wrappers' descriptions to 9,000.

**A3, the proof field.** Verified: decision record 0006; `proof` with 29 cases; `fix-a-bug.md` marked proven with M1's record and M2's closing recordings pinned at `b754e39`, an ancestor of `main`; the README's table, held by the check; `building: ["M3"]` in the manifest; `direct_push` refuses an anchor change pushed straight to main. The sentence about playbooks now reads "a playbook marked proven stays only while it beats the bare model" in `workspace.json`, the `eval_record` docstring and the README.

Every grader passed in 41, 18 and 0 of 45 runs (fix-a-bug 14, 15, 0; nightly-job 15, 0, 0; big-report 12, 3, 0). Guided against two-line p = 2.1e-07. **Proposed revisions (template rule 7).**

- Criterion 55 lists guard refusals that only C3 and C5 can show. A0 meets its parser side; the refusal cases belong to criteria 38, 39, 58 and 65 when those slices land.
- The README promised the commit check starts three git processes. The history the proof and eval checks read adds at most three more, one batch of older files for each check and one ancestry test per anchor commit, whatever the number of files. The README, `docs/parts.md` and the test now say so.
- A plain `check`, with no base, warns that the guided arm was not compared with its past, as A0's row says it should. The warning shows on every plain `check` of a workspace with a proven playbook.

### B0, the port ledger

Built at the worker tier, 2026-11-21, from the private port catalog only; no file of the work workspace was opened. The ledger, its check and the walled-reader brief sit beside the catalog in the maintainer's private repository, not here. This entry and the milestone row are the only changes to this repository. No file the eval hashes was touched.

- **Verified, criteria 16, 24 and 63:** the ledger's check derives 200 items from the catalog and this plan, and prints 0 unmapped. They are 79 procedures and 17 pieces of machinery the batch table gives M3, 18 workspace decisions, 40 lessons, 11 disagreements (the catalog's ten and the eleventh B5 names), 3 ideas kept from left-out items, 24 items the red team found missing (round 1 rows 17 and 18, R2-26, R3-17 and R4-7), and 8 entries too thin to build from (row 20). The check reads the catalog's own tables for the list, so an item added there shows up unmapped.
- **Verified:** a target is a file that exists here (the check confirms it), a slice of this plan, a milestone from M4 to M12, a reason of four words or more, or a dated "struck by the maintainer". By main target: 32 items map to pages that exist now, 145 to a slice, 20 to a later milestone, and 3 to a reason. The check's self-test shows it fails on a blank target, a deleted row, a missing page, an unknown slice, a one-word reason and a duplicate row, and counts a struck row as mapped.
- **Inferred:** criterion 24 is met in the ledger now, and in public only once B5 writes the reasons. Of the 40 lessons, 29 name the rule or part `why` B5 is to write, 9 wait for M4's or M5's machinery, and 2 are retired with a reason.
- **Ordered, not run:** the walled-reader pass for the 8 thin entries: the fix brief's sections, the test-matrix axes, the handoff note's sections, the unit-doc headings per kind, the layout's fixed headings, the security-review surfaces, and two one-line entries. The maintainer starts that session. B2 waits for it, as row 20 requires.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **The two one-line entries.** Row 20 does not name them. B0 chose the standup log and the definition of done's list of obligations: the shortest M3 entries whose structure a builder can find nowhere else in the catalog.
- **"The layout's fixed headings" is ambiguous.** Two catalog entries could be meant, so the brief asks the reader to name the page before describing it, rather than guess.
- **Two items inside the batch table's M3 range were built in M1:** the hand-off plan specification and the tier policy. They map to their existing pages.
- **Disagreements are mapped, not settled.** Each goes to B5's record; the ledger notes only which pages a choice will touch.

**Proposed revision (template rule 7):** the B0 row and V5 say the ledger sits "outside every repository". The catalog it sits beside is in the maintainer's private repository, so the ledger is too, as the maintainer directed. The wording should read "outside every public repository".

### B1, the instruction review

Pull request #32, on branch `m3/b1` from main at `166bbc7`, merged as `ffbdc3d`, at the driver tier. Both runs of the review were made by fresh helper sessions at the driver tier, given the pages and the diff and none of the author's reasoning. No file the eval hashes was edited.

- **Verified, criterion 17:** `review_trailer`, 19 cases, and `tests/test_instruction_review.py`, 19 tests on throwaway repositories with a bare remote. At the commit-msg hook's own command, a staged guidance page with no trailer fails; a listed skip reason passes; a named record that is missing, or on disk but not staged, fails; a staged record that covers the page passes; a deletion needs a trailer; a merge in progress is left to the push and CI. At `check --push`, a push to main holding one commit without a trailer is refused, naming only that commit, and a push to another branch is left to CI. `check --commits BASE..HEAD` fails a range holding such a commit, fails a record committed a commit late, runs `commit_subject` over every commit too, and judges a merge by its own changes. Commits whose own tree does not use the part are not judged, so a branch started before B1 merged keeps its old commits. A test holds the CI step's range to the pull request's base and head, and to the push's before and after.
- **Verified, criterion 17, on this branch:** the live commit-msg hook accepted the second commit, whose ten trailers name the ten records it adds, and `python3 ws.py check --commits origin/main..HEAD` passed on the branch.
- **Pending, criterion 17:** CI reporting a failing run on main after a push that holds such a commit. The step runs on every push to main; no such push has been made.
- **Verified, criterion 20:** `tests/test_tutorials.py`, 8 tests, runs every tutorial's `<!-- runnable -->` blocks in a fresh clone of the commit; with none yet it passes and prints `0 tutorial(s) at HEAD`. On fixture repositories it passes passing blocks, with `python3` started through the clone's launcher; fails a failing block and runs none after it; fails a marker with no block and a tutorial with no marked block. The pre-push hook refuses a push to main that changes a failing tutorial, and passes a passing one. CI runs the file on Linux, macOS and Windows.
- **Verified, criterion 62:** `review_index`, 13 cases. Eight records cover the pages sub-batch A wrote: `AGENTS.md`, `INDEX.md`, the layout page, the glossary, decision 0006, and the rules tables on the machinery, definition-of-done and orchestration pages. The index lists them, `required` holds that list, and a test holds `required` to criterion 62.
- **Verified, the first run, sub-batch A's pages:** 31 findings in 8 records. 18 were fixed, on the layout, glossary, orchestration and machinery pages and by a dated Amended paragraph on decision 0006. 7 are queued for slice Z, since `AGENTS.md`, `INDEX.md` and the definition of done are hashed. 2 are deferred to B2 and C4, 3 are queued for the maintainer, and 1 was declined.
- **Verified, the second run, this slice's pages:** 22 findings in 10 records. 20 were fixed, including two code changes: a record now covers exactly one page, and a tutorial with no marked block fails. 1 is queued for slice Z, an index row for the playbook, and 1 for the maintainer.
- **Inferred, the stop rule:** it did not fire. It counts pages ported in B2's batches, and these pages were not. Contradictions were found in 5 of the 8 pages sub-batch A wrote, well above a third; that rate is queued for the maintainer as a question on B2's rewrite brief.
- **Verified:** `python3 ws.py selftest`, `python3 ws.py check`, `check --phase ci --changed-from origin/main`, `check --history --changed-from origin/main` and `python3 -m unittest discover -b tests` (526 tests, 3 skipped) pass locally; `python3 tests/hook_cases.py` passes 36 of 36; `python3 evals/grade.py --recorded` finds no difference.
- **Verified:** one fix round. On both Windows jobs, the tutorial runner's `bash` was Windows' launcher for its Linux subsystem, which has no distribution there. It now walks up from `git` to Git's own `bash.exe`, as the hook case runner does.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A guidance page is set by path, in the manifest:** the contract and its pointer files, `INDEX.md`, the README, `evals/README.md`, `plugin/` for B6's wrappers, and `docs/` minus plans, milestone records, records and the question log. A test holds the lists to every page's kind.
- **The named record must be added or changed by the same commit, and cover each page.** The row asks only that the record exist. Presence alone would let any old record excuse a new edit, the gap round 1's finding 21 named.
- **The pre-push check judges pushes to main only.** A branch's commits are judged by CI on its pull request, and a new branch's push would otherwise list the whole history.
- **A commit is judged only once its own tree uses the part,** so slices in flight when B1 merges are not failed for commits made before it.
- **The trailer is read anywhere in the message,** not only in git's last paragraph; the commits page asks for the last paragraph, as prose.
- **`loaded_size` now names the layout page,** which holds its rule, as the first review found.

Proposed revisions (template rule 7):

- Criterion 20 says the blocks run green "on three systems". B2's tutorials must then run in Git's bash on Windows. A block that starts Python gets the clone's launcher, so `python3 ws.py check` works there; a block that needs another tool must be one Git for Windows ships.
- Criterion 62's list leaves out `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, which sub-batch A also added. It has no record. The README and the index now say the required list is the plan's.

Proposed fixes to the rewrite brief, for the maintainer's decision before batch 2 (queued as Q9 in the workspace queue):

4. **Builders of one batch read each other's drafts** before they report.

A page commits its own work, such as a question-log row, on whatever branch is checked out, and procedure endings then pushes a worktree's pages with `git push origin HEAD:main`, which carries a code branch's commits to main. The fix round gave four pages an exception off main, in two wordings, and missed three. This is the cause of B3's three stops too, and only procedure endings can state the rule once; the slice's row does not cover that page.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A review run off main pushes nothing.** Architecture review, the review orchestrator and defect escape analysis commit on the branch checked out and push only from main, so no push of pages carries a branch's code to main. Git-host operations instead leaves its question-log row uncommitted off main. Both are labelled exceptions to procedure endings, since this slice does not edit that page.

Decisions made in the slice:

- **A core procedure is a playbook or how-to with no `lane` key, or a lane page once it is marked proven,** read from front matter, so the program's rule that a lane draft gets its wrapper when proven needs no list.
- **A wrapper is named after its page's file, without `.md`,** so federated search's is `search-every-source`. The check holds the name to the page.
- **The selection cases live in `plugin/evals/`,** since `claude plugin eval` reads cases only below the plugin's folder, and the run's results go to `evals/selection/`.
- **The guidance list narrows from `plugin/` to `plugin/skills/`.** The wrappers are guidance; the 116 case files are test inputs, as eval cases are, and the plugin's manifest is wiring. A test holds the split.
- **A case's `input_match` names the plugin as well as the wrapper,** so a skill of the same name from another plugin, such as a person's own handoff skill, does not count.
- **A run is three runs of at most three turns per case, on the small model, with no run that leaves the plugin out,** since a case that fires cannot pass there.
- **The kit syncs `plugin/` and `.claude-plugin/` and seeds `plugin/evals/`,** so an instance rewrites the requests in its own words; the seed README and the seed eval page say so.
- **`plugin/` is not on the machinery list.** The `wrappers` check holds it at the commit and in CI. Whether M4's eval stage makes the cases machinery is M4's.

- B2, `docs/playbooks/fix-a-bug.md`: a section "The fix path's gates", with the line `Proof: unproven, prove-by M6.` under its heading, holding the gates that can run now. First, before any fix, a fix brief of one screen, in ten parts in this order: the target and the commit measured at; each claim of the report, marked verified, corrected or could not verify; the mechanism, and whether the report names the defect or one symptom of it; the fix seam and the seams rejected, always weighing the boundary that admits untrusted input; reuse, in one line; what else the change reaches, from [impact analysis](../playbooks/impact-analysis.md); the test plan, naming the test matrix's axes in play; what is blocked on the person, a decision kept apart from a missing input, each saying what it blocks and with a recommended answer; what the report should have carried; and what is left out on purpose. An empty part is one line saying so. A brief that does not fit one screen is itself the finding, and the work climbs the ladder. Nothing blocking means carry on; something blocking is the stop, with the brief as the report. Second, stop and ask before a migration, a data repair, a configuration change or a change to a shared environment. The test step gains the test matrix: every caller, each kind of signed-in identity, each client platform, empty and populated data and a demonstration user against a real one, the routes a smoke suite covers, and at least one error path; an axis not in play is left out of the brief's test plan, and a browser path with console errors is not verified. The review gates wait for M4 and the tracker gates for M8. One conflict for slice Z to settle (inferred): the ported procedure skips the brief for a small, easy-to-reverse fix, and every fix this page takes passes that gate.

The counts below are verified, from that script; every cause is inferred, since no run has tested one.

- **Verified, where the label was lost:** 19 of the 90 routed runs lost "claim marked verified", 18 of them on that grader alone; R2's guided arm lost it in 1 of 45. In 13 of the 19 reports the word "verified" never appears. In the other 6 it appears only in prose, such as "**Not verified:**" or "verified by running `unittest`". Each of the 19 ends with a summary in sections, such as Cause, Fix, Test, "Choices I made" and "Not done", with no claim marked.
- **Verified, the record path:** 63 of the 90 routed runs opened a fix record under `docs/records/fixes/`, and 15 of the 19 losses are among them. 67 runs read the fix record template, and 5 read the definition of done. In R2's guided arm, no run wrote a record, since the arm has no template, and 16 of 45 read the definition of done. The path starts at the fix path's gates, which slice Z added to `fix-a-bug.md`: "Before step 1, open the fix's record ... as the fix record template says". Step 6 then sends the agent to the template too.
- **Verified, no page read predicts the loss within the routed arm:** runs that wrote a record lost the label in 15 of 63, and runs that did not in 4 of 27 (one-sided Fisher exact test, p = 0.25). Runs that read the template lost it in 15 of 67, and the rest in 4 of 23. Runs that read procedure endings lost it in 11 of 57, and the rest in 8 of 33. Against the guided arm's 1 of 45, the record writers differ at p = 0.0012, and the routed runs with no record at p = 0.06.
- **Verified, the record holds the marks that the reply then leaves out:** one lost report says "The record marks the real-page wiring as inferred". The transcripts keep each edit's path but not its text, so the records themselves cannot be read.
- **Inferred, the cause:** the template gives the marked report a second home. Its "In short" line says the record's `## Report` is marked and that a check reads it. "When to write one" says the record's report "is the report that page ends with". The rule that the reply repeats those lines sits in one clause, under "Filling it in". An agent that has just written marked lines into the record ends the reply with a summary of the work instead. That explains the 15. It does not explain the 4 routed runs that lost the label with no record. They read only the playbook, or the playbook and one other page. What every routed run has and no guided run has is the 28 wrappers in the session and the pages unstripped. 15 routed runs opened by reading the playbook with the Read tool, as the fix-a-bug wrapper asks, and no guided run did; they lost the label in 5 of 15, against 14 of the other 75 (p = 0.18). The transcripts do not record skill calls, so whether a wrapper fired cannot be seen.
- **Verified, not a cause:** branches. 57 routed runs committed on a new branch, as procedure endings asks for code under `src/`. They lost the label in 13 of 57, and the rest in 6 of 33. Naming a missing folder or `ws.py` is not one either: 18 of 23 routed passes with no record name one, and so do all 4 losses.

None of the first three is in a case's hash: each of the three fix-a-bug records covers only `fix-a-bug.md`, the definition of done and, for two cases, the orders-api rule page (verified, each record's `covers`). A fix to any of them keeps R2 current. Each is a guidance page, so each change needs an instruction-review record.

| Page | What leads there | Proposed fix | Hashed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `docs/templates/fix-record.md` | The marked report gets a second home, and the rule that the reply repeats it is one clause deep. | The "In short" line and "When to write one" both say: end your reply with the report, its claims marked, and keep the same lines in the record. The record is a copy and does not replace the reply. | No |
| `docs/procedure-endings.md`, "The report" | Its last sentence lets a procedure write the report "to a record". | It says the reply still ends with the marked lines when a record holds a copy. | No |
| `plugin/skills/fix-a-bug/SKILL.md` | Every routed run has the wrappers in its session. The fix-a-bug wrapper says only "read the whole page and follow it". | The body says to follow the page through its last step, the report. The description stays as it is, so B6's selection result still stands (inferred). | No |
| `docs/playbooks/fix-a-bug.md`, step 6 and the gates section's first line | Both send the agent to the template for the report. | Step 6 says the reply ends with the report and the record holds a copy. | Yes: its three records go stale, so R2 runs again |

Run counts for the remeasure, each against R2's guided 42 of 45:

### GFIX, Q74's narrowed guard rules and git or gh in any case

Draft pull request #56, on branch `m3/gfix` from `m3/z` at `4a22107`, at the driver tier. The approval is GFIX's entry of 2026-11-24 under "Changes after approval", pushed to main as `d164193` before the build. This entry and the milestone record's row are on main; the code, cases, pages and review records are on the branch. It touches no file the eval hashes. It stopped after two fix rounds on escapes in `$'...'` quoting (Q80). The maintainer's answers to Q80 and Q79 gave one more round by exception, fix round 3, which finished the slice; its bullets follow the first run's. 0 eval runs.

- **Verified, the rules, `gitrules.unreadable` in `wslib/gitrules.py` and its copy in `parts/no_admin_merge.py`:** the seven shell guards outside the eval's hash refuse, in their own part files, an ANSI-C escape the shell makes a printable character, a tab or a new line, an octal escape counted by its low eight bits; a NUL escape in a word that holds anything else, and a word of only NUL escapes among the words of `eval` or `watch`; a `((` that may hold a command or a substitution; braces, a glob or an unquoted variable or substitution among a wrapper's or a shell's own words; a wrapper, shell, `eval`, `watch` or `find` inside 8 nested shells; a program the parser does not read, one of whose words is, as written, `git`, `gh`, `env`, `printenv`, `pwsh`, `powershell` or a shell the parser reads; and `git` or `gh` in another letter case (Q75). Commits `2d83108`, `6efbff3` and `9ec2e12`.
- **Verified, no program list:** the runner, data, lookup and interpreter lists, the pager rule and the parsing of runner text are gone. Every program the parser does not read is judged, a wrapper named in another case among them. The runner forms this leaves open, such as `flock /tmp/l -c 'git push -f origin main'` and `arch $(command -v git) push -f origin main`, are now 11 cases that pass, each named as waiting for M4's parser fix.
- **Verified, test first:** every new refusal case failed on the tree before its commit: 23 on `4a22107`, 5 on `2d83108`, 5 on `6efbff3`.

No stored grade changes. The README names the class and the way around it, `command -v`. Parked as Q79, with the recommendation to keep no list until M4.

The first found 11 items, 2 blocking: a NUL escape at the start of a word, and a wrapper named in another case, such as `NICE -n 5 git push -f origin main`. Fix round 1 closed both; its re-check found 2 more: an octal escape past `\377`, and a NUL-only word among `eval`'s words. Refusing every escape in `$'...'` except those the parser reads itself, and a word of only `\0`, ends the hunt; it newly refuses no stored eval command (verified).
